# LLP 3000.000.000.002: Declarative release backfills

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Draft
**Systems:** Snapback 4 language, storage, release adoption, staging clock, proof kit
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (MIG)
**Date:** 2026-10-19
**Revised:** 2026-10-19
**Related:** [Release activation](./3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md), [Staging](./3000.000.000.001-staging-with-real-users.rfc.md)

## 1. Commission

Implementer: MIG, 2026-10-19. Wave 17's fifty changes require constant,
conditional, derived, enum, string-split and point-lookup transformations.
Declare these in Quarry, with no public migration mutation, job or JavaScript.
REL5 owns release selection/restoration and old-client compatibility; SEC3 owns
security checks. No new version, publication or push authority is implied.

## 2. Language

A top-level declaration describes one table's historical input fields and its
simultaneous output patch:

```quarry
backfill bookings vehicleClass(weight: int, length: int):
  { vehicleClass: 'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 500 else 'car' }
```

Here length is stored in centimetres. Arguments are typed historical fields;
they remain in the declaration after the candidate drops those fields. The
compiler binds arguments against their explicit types, not today's table.
The runtime validates them against each interpreted historical row. Output
names belong to candidate authoritative columns and may equal input names:
read-old/write-new. Outputs of multiple declarations on a table must be
disjoint. Names are unique per table. Continuous carry-forward keeps the
same normalized definition; changing it under the same name refuses with a
bridge/new-name repair. A new name denotes a new transformation.

The expression body is an object patch. It uses Quarry's existing arithmetic,
comparisons, null handling, conditional expressions, immutable helper expansion,
and bounded deterministic scalar builtins. Supported text additions are
`before(text, delimiter)`, `after`, `beforeLast`, `afterLast`, `startsWith`,
and `allDigits`. Splits remove the first/last exact delimiter; absent delimiter
means whole text before, empty text after. An empty split delimiter refuses.
Digits means nonempty ASCII digits. Text is Unicode, comparisons and splits
are locale independent. Existing `lower`, `trim`, `slice`, `count`, `sha256`,
`first`, `last`, `at`, `min`, `max`, exact `divide`, calendar/civil transforms
and concatenation retain their normal checked semantics. No randomness,
viewer, clock, minted ids, writes, mutation helpers or loops are admitted.
Only scalar outputs are admitted, including new references. Assets, lists,
engine-owned/captured/maintained and lifetime outputs refuse. Overwriting an
existing reference/asset is refused: derive a new reference column so the
existing dormant reference-lifetime ledger owns retirement/restoration.

Point reads use the ordinary `members[principal = owner] ! else NOT_FOUND`
spelling. They require a unique point. A missing asserted point refuses;
ordinary nullable point reads and `exists` retain their explicit semantics.
Historical existence predicates may traverse a metered source range, needed
for wave-17 cross-tower references; they return no row collection. A meter
cut is a refusal, never false absence or an incomplete historical value.
All reads run as the trusted activation system principal, bypassing application
read rules, against the assembled historical snapshot. Dormant namespaces
remain unnameable. Candidate read indexes are built over that source snapshot
only when their component fields exist there. No JavaScript or app migration
operation receives this authority. All declarations are self-contained: they
cannot read another declaration's outputs in the same activation.

## 3. Adoption and reversibility

The existing activation writer transaction owns this sequence:

1. Assemble retained source rows/cells and their source definitions using the
   existing restoration intent. Retire source-expired lifetimes before any
   backfill; a transform cannot extend an expiry or opening boundary.
2. When leaving a declaration, restore its predecessor-visible output fields
   from saved **output preimages**, separately from its argument inputs. Save
   interpreted old values, including literal defaults/nulls, and absence.
   Added output columns use ordinary dormant retention instead. A different
   current value from both recorded output and preimage refuses with a conflict;
   untouched same-column enum renames restore their exact original value.
3. Enter declarations absent from the immediately active schema. Unchanged
   carry-forward and reopening run none: candidate-native writes are not
   historical rows. On re-entry, restored cells/rows are eligible along with
   older active rows. A native v2 row whose absent historical inputs have a
   complete valid restored output image keeps that image. Older v1 rows lacking
   outputs are filled. Declarations must accompany skipped-release source;
   no unseen intermediate transformation is implicitly inferred.
4. Evaluate every entering patch against one assembled source snapshot, with
   ordinary per-row job meters and a paged source walk. Stage output/evidence
   in SQLite temp storage before applying any patch. Recompute the patch on
   re-entry, so changed lookup values count as changed dependencies even when
   declared arguments are unchanged. Compare both saved argument values and
   computed outputs. If unchanged, preserve edited added-column cells. If
   changed, recompute only when current outputs equal the saved result or the
   reversed preimage; otherwise refuse the conflict. Never overwrite a manual
   output edit because another input happened to change.
5. Apply patches and evidence together. Run ordinary narrowing/reference
   closure, projection, complete type/asset validation, indexes, uniqueness,
   caps and maintained-data rebuilds. Transformed dependencies force rebuilds
   even when their declarations did not change. Publish programs, generation
   and release in the same commit. Any error rolls everything back.

Evidence is keyed by table number, row lifetime and the SHA-256 digest of the
normalized declaration/typed IR (no diagnostic locations or whitespace).
It records declared input values, each overwritten output preimage and computed
output. Ordinary authoritative deletion/expiry deletes that row's evidence in
its transaction; id reuse cannot inherit it. Row moves to dormant storage
preserve lifetime. Backfill definitions enter schema/activation identity;
changed rows also set activation population, forcing a fresh generation and
device reacquisition. Public/device schema projections omit all backfill
metadata; devices never execute migrations. A successful evidence installation
sets `snapback4-release-store-4`, transactionally. Unaware binaries refuse that
format even when an old source is selected. Failed installation retains the
previous format. Legacy stores without backfills remain unchanged.

Dropping a declaration has explicit semantic meaning: leave it by restoring
preimages or refusing conflicts. Keep declarations in every later source
whose view should retain their meaning. Refusals include exact affected counts
and up to five bounded privileged samples. Missing inputs/lookups, output/type
errors, arithmetic/meter errors and uniqueness/cap conflicts refuse atomically;
a widened/optional non-unique bridge plus an authorized repair mutation is the
repair path. A post-backfill-edit conflict requires keeping the current/bridge
source, deciding a truthful old representation, then repairing it before
selecting old source. No automatic fallback turns a new enum value into an old
meaning. Native v2 rows with no preimage retain the parent's existing law:
forward selection may make incompatible present values dormant; rollback
requires representability and refuses with its existing reason. This amendment
does not change REL5's rollback or restoration selection policy.

`deploy --check` uses the existing private disk snapshot, deploy direction and
restoration intent; rollback previews use rollback direction. Later live writes
can change its plan. `backfills` in both cold/hot JSON receipts and exact preview
text reports `table name transformed N rows` or `table name reversed on N rows`.
These are transformed row visits, separate from the parent's disjoint dormant
rows/cells/index entries. They are not additive counts of distinct rows across
different transformations, and do not claim a frozen production population.

## 4. Staging clock

Only a store durably classified staging by deploy --staging may serve with
--clock-offset. Missing/unknown classification is production. This setting
is process-local, with the same signed-duration parser and monotone elapsed
clock as dev. No serve-time mechanism can reclassify an existing production store. Restart drops the offset unless explicitly
provided again; persisted timed-disclosure floors never retreat below their captured value.

Staging exposes the existing /dev/clock/status and /dev/clock/advance only
with any full service session: these are trusted operator credentials, as for
system jobs. Production refuses even with a service token, and an explicit
production serve offset refuses with E_CLOCK_DEV_ONLY before listener startup. Account, guest, read-only and unauthenticated
requests refuse. Recheck classification and session under the engine writer
before advancing. All fixture, reset, persona and dev-job doors remain closed.
Use the same advance implementation, schedule drain and ambiguous-reply law
as dev. CLI clock advance --release DIR mints its local service authority.
startServe accepts clockOffset and exposes now()/advance() through that
operator authority; it never changes store classification.

## 5. Evidence

Tests cover all fifty historical shapes, no-op/restart, v1 writes during
rollback, same-column enum reversal, edited-output refusal, missing lookup,
invalid output, uniqueness failure, deletion/id reuse, generation reset and
hot/cold/preview equivalence. The guide's persistent production recipe uses
real users and operator role setup, tests tightened privacy and declares its
invariant, rolls back and forward with business assertions. Its exact fences
run against the fresh packed tarball. Reproduce five copied bakeoff lanes,
record actual line/time deltas. Run required crate/client/differential/Linux,
Mac Studio clock tests, refs, budget and root checks.

This revision specifies historical eligibility, exact preimages, lookup changes,
phase order, immutable declaration identities, reference/asset restrictions,
format/generation fences and bounded bridge repairs. It retains strict conflict
refusal rather than Muse's suggested silent edited-row preservation: preserving
a different same-column value under old semantics could change business meaning.
The author explicitly permits rollback refusal with a reason.

reproduction and measured results are recorded in its report.

# llp-orient

<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0008-distributed-agent-skills.rfc.md#the-skills--core-five-plus-flags — skill contract -->
<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0000-linked-literate-programming.explainer.md#2-reference-syntax — the grammar this reads -->

Load the design rationale governing the area you're about to touch, before touching it. This is the consumption side of LLP — orienting first is what turns `@ref` from a possibility into a habit.

Invoke as `/llp-orient <path-or-task>` (file, directory, subsystem, or task description). With no argument, use the current change scope (`git diff --name-only`).

## Trigger

Start of a coding task that touches an area with documented design. The orient-first *rule* lives in the repo's always-loaded `AGENTS.md`; this skill is the detailed form that rule routes to for non-trivial work — don't rely on ambient description-match alone.

## Invariants

- MUST be read-only: never edit code or LLP documents.
- MUST scope to the task: pull the constraints that bind this change, not a corpus summary.
- MUST cite, not restate: reference policy as `LLP NNNN#anchor` rather than paraphrasing sections.
- MUST NOT invent constraints. If something isn't documented, say so.
- MUST NOT guess the rationale behind a broken `@ref` — report it and hand off to `llp-maintain`.

## Workflow

> **Recipe (advisory)** — corpus root = nearest `llp/` walking up from the path in scope (LLP 0001 §Unit of adoption); "no corpus" is an empty walk, not cwd-relative absence. Read `llp/foundation/` first (the kernel; the root LLP is there once `Active` — on a fresh adoption it is still `Draft` and sits in `current/`), then skim `llp/current/` (the declared working set); both hold relative links — symlinks, or small files containing the relative path — so `ls` answers and either form resolves. Then the root LLP's systems vocabulary. `Grep '@ref'` over the files in scope and `Read` each target — these are the highest-signal context. `Grep` the `**Systems:**` headers across `llp/` for LLPs governing the subsystems touched; read the governing ones (Spec, Decision, Principles, relevant RFCs). Follow `Related:` and nearby `@ref` targets one hop out; stop there unless something is clearly load-bearing. Stale-pin nudge (skip offline): read the installed tag from the skills receipt (or a `source:` pin), compare `git ls-remote --tags <origin>` — if upstream is newer, say so in one line ("skills at v0.3.0, upstream v0.5.2 — run /llp-adopt update") and move on; never update from here.

## Artifact

A context pack with these sections, faster to read than the documents it points at:

- **Scope** — files / subsystems queried
- **Governing LLPs** — numbered list, one-line gloss each; mark which are in `foundation/` and which are in `current/` (in-flight — its guidance may still move)
- **Active constraints** — bullets, each citing `LLP NNNN#anchor`
- **Relevant rationale** — the *why*, same citation form
- **Open questions / risks** for this area
- **Likely doc updates** if the change lands
- **Hand-off** — one of `none | llp-adopt | llp-create | llp-review | llp-maintain`

## Hand-offs

- No corpus (no `llp/` walking up from the path in scope) → `llp-adopt`.
- The task needs a decision no LLP covers → `llp-create` (or `llp-review` if proposal-shaped).
- An `@ref` target or anchor doesn't resolve → `llp-maintain`.
# Snapback 4 — agent command sheet

Read this, then [LLP 3000](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the root and
the bakeoff protocol), [LLP 2000.000](../llp/2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md)
(the design brief), [`NOT-DOING.md`](./NOT-DOING.md), and the tail of
[`JOURNAL.md`](./JOURNAL.md). `rules/RULES.md` at the repository root is
adopted here as written; LLPs for Snapback 4 are numbered **3000 and up**
(`llp-create --under 3000` for a child; extend LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000
before minting a new number).

```
cargo build                          # the binary; no JS engine, no bootstrap; crates.io plus one vendored patch (vendor/tiny_http)
scripts/test.sh                      # everything blocking (60 s budget); reports every failure, never fail-fast
./target/debug/snapback4 --help      # init [--online|--expo] · check [--cost|--residency|--fix|--json] · dev · deploy · rollback · serve · run (an op, or a job as the system) · test · why · guide · data · sql · token (a service's seat) · doctor · device · types · --version
./target/debug/snapback4 guide       # lists cards (`guide aliases` lists aliases); `guide language` for the language card — every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite (crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs)
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 check     # compiles snapback/*.q, writes snapback/generated/api.ts
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 dev       # serves 127.0.0.1:4400; re-adopts the schema on save
cd apps/chat && bun run dev                            # the app on the real client (?as=bob picks a persona)
bun run --cwd packages/snapback4 typecheck   # the contract, the client and the hooks typecheck
cargo test -p snapback4-core --test corpus -- --nocapture   # the conformance corpus, with skip reasons
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest     # fixed seeds, oracle regressions and replay (opt-in, no database by default)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- compile case.q   # a generated or hand-written source's binder diagnostics ("ok" when none)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 6 --out /tmp/quarry-difftest
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/rewrite-identity--why--NOT_FOUND
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-devices --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-devices   # server against both device stores; no database
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/device-sqlite--write-predicted-refused--admitted
(cd lean && lake build) && cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-lean --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-lean   # the engine against the Lean model (LLP 3000.035 §5.2); ~900 cases/s on 8 workers
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay 'crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/lean--kind--committed~NOT_FOUND'   # needs the model built, or SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE=<quarry-oracle>
./target/debug/snapback4 check --emit-lean schema.q   # one checked file as the Lean model's case JSON; programs outside its fragment listed with a reason
# Store tests require SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES pointing to a private evaluation cluster.
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres  # fails if the cluster is absent
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- run-stores --seed 0 --cases 1000000 --workers 8 --seconds 10800 --out /tmp/quarry-stores
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- replay /path/to/found-case
python3 scripts/test-difftest-scheduled.py  # runner/ticket tests; no schedule or push
scripts/difftest-scheduled.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner" --state-dir "$HOME/.snapback-quarry-difftest" --hours 3
# Operator only: install daily launchd (Darwin) or user systemd timer (Linux).
scripts/install-difftest-schedule.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner"
cargo test -p snapback4-sim          # the chat world under faults: the seven invariants
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive   # every sequence of two writes (two viewers, two groups): disclosure and write authority against a model of the rules
SNAPBACK4_EXHAUSTIVE_DEPTH=3 cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive -- --ignored   # three writes: ~7,000 states, minutes
scripts/kani.sh [harness...]         # Kani proofs of the key encoding (crates/snapback4-core/src/key_proofs.rs; minutes, ~12 GB peak); outside test.sh
(cd lean && ./check.sh)              # the Lean model of Quarry's core (LLP 3000.035 §5.2): lake build (~15 s cold, needs elan), no-sorry/axiom audit, 53 examples, the quarry-oracle I/O fixture; outside test.sh; open points in lean/OPEN.md
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test native   # native jobs: builds native-fixtures/rescore for wasm32-unknown-unknown (cold ~20 s, then cached under target/native-fixture) and runs it
bash scripts/build-wasm.sh           # the device as wasm into packages/snapback4/src/wasm/ (the web tier and the Chromium witness need it); fails when the gzip exceeds metrics/wasm-budget.json — a landing that needs the bytes raises the budget in the same commit, with the reason
node witness/idb.mjs                 # the Chromium witness: the client on the wasm device over IndexedDB (needs Chrome)
WITNESS_UDID=<dedicated-udid> node witness/expo.mjs   # published witness verb/helper; freshly install apps/chat-expo Release first (simctl uninstall a previous build: its store belongs to an earlier backend); port 4400, no Metro
node witness/scale.mjs [G] [N] [M]   # cold sync at scale: G groups × N messages × M members into the wasm device; wall time + JS heap → metrics/witness.jsonl
node scripts/measure-storage.mjs     # what the store weighs on the scale seed (rows, indexes, partition log) → metrics/storage.jsonl
                                     # SNAPBACK4_BIN=target/release/snapback4 measures the release build (throughput numbers)
node scripts/measure-client.mjs      # each client tier's bytes on the pinned toolchain → metrics/client.jsonl
node scripts/attribute-wasm.mjs [--families] [--check-families] [--check-absent] # class bytes and concrete serde families; --check-families refuses non-text readers/non-Vec writers outside the exact dynamic-envelope, counting-writer and Scan map-replay allowlists; content-buffer families name the duplicated types and refuse Deserialize representations using untagged, tag or flatten; --check-absent refuses the core's prefetch, admission or log-event symbols in the web artifact (needs twiggy; slow, outside test.sh)
bash scripts/check-release-guard.sh # the web artifact's configuration plus the debug-only `web-device-check` feature must refuse to build (in test.sh)
bash scripts/measure-wasm.sh         # the device core as wasm (crates/snapback4-device), family-checked, bound and measured → metrics/wasm.jsonl
bash scripts/build-xcframework.sh    # the device as an iOS xcframework for packages/snapback4-expo (staticlib alone, stripped; 13.2 MB with native reads)
node loopbar/run.mjs --n 3           # the loop bar: fresh agents on the published pin build reduced Clique M1–M3 → metrics/loopbar.jsonl
node scripts/publish.mjs --prepare-client # rebuild wasm + clean client, pack once for cross-host qualification
# Linux qualification host: strace >=6.8 on PATH (audits every successful TCP listen).
node scripts/qualify-linux.mjs --client /path/client.tgz --expo /path/expo.tgz --out /path/qualification # Linux suite + Ubuntu/Alpine proof, no publish
# Add --consumer-only for packed frontend/toolchain proofs without test.sh; this writes consumer.json, never a publication receipt.
node scripts/publish.mjs             # Mac: qualify/sign Darwin; optional --linux-receipt + --linux-tarball import; platforms before client
```

Crates: `snapback4-core` (value model, schema with `sync`, IR, key encoding,
SQLite store, rules with hoisting, the meter, the interpreter, the partition
log, at-most-once, the day-two classifier, wire forms), `snapback4-lang`
(Quarry lexer, parser, binder, diagnostics, the TypeScript emitter),
`snapback4` (verbs and the HTTP wire), `snapback4-sim` (the deterministic
world hosting the engine and a replica device), `snapback4-difftest` (seeded
Quarry source generator, rewrite and meter oracles, optional Postgres store
oracles with prefetch on/off, the server-against-device oracle on both device stores, the engine-against-Lean-model oracle, structural shrinking and replay; not published or linked by shipped crates), `snapback4-device` (the
interpreter over a `DeviceStore`: the core's SQLite under the native
module for Expo and Exact2 hosts, memory under wasm on the web; one
dispatcher, one suite on both)). The guide (`snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs`) is
the language card and the keyword cards; packages: `snapback4` (the client
over the device and the CLI shim), `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and `snapback4-linux-x64` (the binaries), `snapback4-expo`
(the Swift module over the device's C ABI).

What exists, by week (LLP 3000 §5):

| Week | Lands |
|---|---|
| 1 | the contract (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`), the mocked chat app against it, the hub fixture wrapper, the five floor measurements in the journal, the simulator skeleton (`crates/snapback4-sim`), the Snapback 2 corpus copied in, 0.0.1 published |
| 2 | server: SQLite transaction and interpreter, rules and uniformity, the meter, the partition log (`sync`), generations, personas, the wire, `check`/`dev`/`run`/`test`/`why`/`guide`, the seven simulator invariants |
| 3 | web client: replica store, outbox with client-minted ids, local interpreter, reactive local queries, the online-only tier, the chat app on the real client |
| 4 | Expo client, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop bar, the Clique hub run, the comparison record |

Gotchas you cannot derive from the code:

- Own Cargo and Bun workspaces; the repository root excludes them. Run cargo/bun from `snapback4/`. Node 24.x (`engines`): run the suite with the pinned runtime on PATH, not the ambient Homebrew node.
- Web builds require `bun install --frozen-lockfile` first. Binaryen 132.0.0 is
  an exact workspace devDependency; build/measurement invoke its portable
  `node_modules/binaryen/bin/wasm-opt` through Node, never a PATH optimizer.
  Missing/mismatched dependencies and optimizer failures refuse the build.
  See README's web-device build instructions and HV round 4 for host parity.
- The suite schedules client files in one pool: `max(1, min(24,
  floor(os.availableParallelism() / 3)))` slots (3 on 10 CPUs, 24 on 96).
  This counts Linux CPU affinity and leaves room for each file's servers,
  browser renderers and workers. Acquisition and server-e2e share that pool;
  there are no extra shard processes outside it. Real-timer files listed in
  `TIMER_CLIENT_FILES` drain that pool and run individually afterward with
  unchanged timing bounds. The quote-jump browser witness remains pooled.
  The two full-count model
  files retain a separate serial slot. `SNAPBACK4_TEST_SERIAL_CLIENT_FILES=1`
  still forces one client file at a time. The external file watchdog measures
  time since a subtest started or finished: max(900 seconds, largest single
  declared guard + max(60 seconds, 25%)). There is no absolute duration cap.
  Console output does not count as progress. Serial mode uses the same bounds.
  See `scripts/run-client-tests.mjs` and the QF round-2 guard census; update
  named work counts and recheck the census when declarations change. Every
  file runs as native `node --test` with default isolation, in a process group
  whose first member is a tiny Node keeper. The runner closes the keeper's stdin
  on completion, stall or abort; only the keeper calls `kill(0, SIGKILL)`.
  Runner death also closes that pipe. An unexpectedly exited keeper fails the
  file and never authorizes a runner signal. Cleanup requires two consecutive
  completed inspections (50 ms apart) with no living group member. Observed leftovers
  fail as TEST_FILE_LEAK. Failed listings and unclassifiable PID/PPID/PGID
  prefixes remain fail-closed. A ps timeout at two seconds retries once with a
  60-second bound; all other command errors refuse immediately. Listings use
  asynchronous spawn so other files' timers and keeper EOF delivery keep running.
  File watchdogs and cleanup's five-second window use per-file monotonic time.
  Expiry requires another completed snapshot: a live group after the deadline
  refuses; two consecutive empty snapshots pass even when the second is late.
  Precisely
  recognized exit-time routing anomalies
  discard the whole snapshot and retry at most twice, 50 ms apart; persistent
  anomalies refuse and never confer ownership (LLP 3000 §7, QF2).
  Metadata is validated for group members and
  their visible descendants; unrelated rows only increment diagnostics.
  Signed Darwin UIDs, variable lstart spacing and comm spaces are accepted.
  Vanished members count as exited. Processes leaving the group (setsid,
  setpgid or detached spawns) are outside cleanup custody; visible descendants
  receive metadata validation but are never signalled by the runner. Native Node's exit
  status alone decides test success; reporter counts are diagnostic only.
  The reporter observes progress as Node delivers it: synchronous work that
  does not yield may buffer events in Node's child until that work finishes.
  Such an unreported interval is subject to the same stall bound. See QF Round 7.
  The M5 MacBook is the publication host; the office M1 is calibration-only.
- Client tests and their helpers must gate Linux APIs (`/proc`, `strace`, `ss`,
  `taskset`, namespace/network tools) at the test or call, or implement a
  portable fallback. Keep portable assertions running on Darwin and give each
  skipped witness an explicit reason. The client pool runs
  `test-platform-audit.test.mjs`: a cheap static census follows static named/namespace `.mjs`
  imports and named helpers and checks scoped Linux skips/branches/early returns.
  A gate in another test does not count. This conservative common-case guard
  does not prove dynamic imports, generated scripts or arbitrary control flow;
  review those paths and run affected tests on the publication Mac.
  Listener snapshots and syscall tracing are Linux qualification capabilities,
  never dependencies of the published `snapback4/proof` browser helper.
- Before a publication dry run, warm the test artifacts **in that publication
  worktree**, while the host is free of calibration work:
  `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT cargo test --workspace --no-run`.
  Use the dry run's toolchain, target directory, profile/features, `RUSTFLAGS`
  and Cargo profile environment. Do not add `--release` or `--profile production`:
  the suite uses the default test profile. The suite still asks Cargo for the
  complete test inventory, runs every binary and runs doctests separately.
  Warming compiles without executing tests and supplies no qualification receipt.
- One owner on the core (LLP 3000 decision 4): the engine, the store and the client contract are written by the lead session; Codex lanes take the fixture generator, the reference apps, the device witnesses and the periphery. Do not reshape `contract.ts` from a lane; propose the change in the journal.
- No ceiling is raised in slice one (LLP 2000.000 §12). A thing that does not fit changes the design or waits.
- The journal is append-only and dated; never rewrite an entry. LLP 3000 §7 carries decisions that must outlive it.
- Nothing here depends on `snapback2/` at runtime. Copy source deliberately, split under 1,500 lines on the way in, and say so in the journal.
- Named lane: `~/projects/snapback-sb4` on `sb4/*` branches. Never `git stash`; commit checkpoints.
- The conformance corpus (`corpus/`) is Snapback 2's serialized IR and value
  model; the core keeps those serializations so it binds. A case the slice
  cannot run is skipped with a named reason, never counted as a pass.
- `snapback/generated/api.ts` is built by `check` and `dev`, never committed;
  the app's typecheck needs a `check` first (the suite does one).
- Point rule probes and `exists` require a unique key. A fully pinned superset
  of a required unique key is unique too (for example an explicit role index
  containing the unique conversation/principal pair). Nullable unique key
  arguments need non-null proof. Other existence checks use bounded populations
  in code. Named equalities covering a total unique key check extra columns on
  that one row without an additional index.
- The packaged CLI uses `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT` during both publication tests and its production build (LLP 3000 §7). A plain production Cargo build retains the dependency's ordinary SQLite policy; use the same build input when comparing with the package. The variable is unnecessary at runtime.
- When creating a stage, `scripts/publish.mjs` runs the suite and refuses on red, builds the production binary, packs `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and verifies the installed digest, then publishes both packages. Bump `version` in `packages/snapback4/package.json` (and its `optionalDependencies`), `packages/snapback4-darwin-arm64/package.json`,
  `packages/snapback4-linux-x64/package.json` and `Cargo.toml` together; a test refuses a mismatch.
- Publication retains exact qualified tarballs under `target/publication/`. If a
  stage exists, use `node scripts/publish.mjs --resume` to validate its recorded
  suite qualification, requalify those exact bytes through the installed-consumer
  proof, and finish missing registry artifacts; an existing version must match
  its staged integrity. This also applies after `--dry-run`. An ordinary invocation
  refuses an existing stage so changed source cannot silently publish old bytes.
  Bump the version to build a new pair; preserve a partially published stage.
- Cross-host publication (LLP 3000.021 §3.3): prepare the common client once
  from clean release source, qualify that tarball on Linux, and import the
  Linux tarball plus its receipt on the Mac. Source commit, client SRI and
  Linux SRI must agree. Linux prefers working Docker/Podman, otherwise uses checksum-pinned proot
  with clean Ubuntu 24.04/Alpine root filesystems in a user cache, and refuses
  `NPM_TOKEN` or `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`, even when empty. It never signs or packs
  the client. The Mac never rebuilds or executes Linux; `--resume` rechecks
  its copied receipt. No receipt packs a Darwin-only client; an existing
  two-platform preparation is refused on that path. Packed dependencies must
  match the staged set. Uploads use verified private snapshots, with each
  digest checked again immediately before publish.
  Commands and current qualification limits:
  [`reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md`](./reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md).
- The guide is tested text. Every ```quarry block in `crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs` and in `packages/snapback4/README.md` is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs` with no diagnostic and no note (the language card is also run against the engine); every refusal code the sources emit must be in `guide::CODES`; every `guide("..")` a diagnostic carries must name a card. Add a form to the language card when you add it to the parser, fence README Quarry as ```quarry, and never fence Quarry as plain text to dodge the test.
- Native jobs (LLP 3000.005) need `rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown`; the suite's fixture build fails loudly without it. The guest SDK `crates/snapback4-native` is a path dependency until it is published; an app's `snapback/native/Cargo.toml` names it by path.

Quarry store testing (LLP 3000.035 §5.1): `SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES` is an
explicit evaluation connection. The factory owns one physical schema per worker,
restores its rows and bookkeeping, installs the compiled fixture, then opens the
ordinary engine. Mutation attempts restore a seeded snapshot; shrinking reuses
that same factory. `Database::connect_fresh()` is an opt-in test mode. Fresh and
restored outcomes must serialize to identical bytes. The local wire observer
retains SQLSTATE, message, detail and SQL statement before the public query API
converts a driver error to a refusal; it never changes the traffic.

Reads sweep every public boundary. Mutations compare every discovered position
twice on SQLite, and compare up to eight seeded positions per dimension on
Postgres (including first/last), plus the case's original budget. Sampling
counters are in `store_stats`; each selected position runs prefetch on and off.
Physical prefetch counters are separate from the exact public outcome record.
The widened generator (`src/widen.rs`) emits prefetch-reachable reads: pages
projecting `tags[.tag]` and `tags[name = .label]` (a `Get` and a unique
`Lookup` whose keys vary across rows) and a loop reading `tags[row.tag]`, so
`store_stats` reports marked reads, transferred rows, cases with a transfer
and `prefetch_consumption_witness_probes` (fewer database calls with prefetch
on: cached answers were consumed). A planted cached-hit-to-miss fault is caught
by the random corpus (LLP 3000.035 §5.1; the closed
[coverage ticket](../issues/closed/20261007-quarry-store-oracle-prefetch-coverage-gap.md)).
Heap is charged identically on every store and the device (LLP 3000.007
§20.24.5); any disagreement is a ticket candidate, never normalized into a pass.
The cluster regression asserts it finds no signature and refuses any new one.

`run-stores --seconds N` stops each worker between cases after N seconds; a case
and any shrinking already underway finish. `cases` remains the requested count,
`generated` is actual completed generation, and `time_limited_workers` reports
the early stop. Slowest-case timing includes generation, comparisons, shrinking
and artifact saving. It does not silently claim the requested million. The scheduled
runner rejects incomplete reports; its own `--hours` option instead chooses a
case count from a 256-case calibration of both campaigns, with 20% headroom and
a million-case cap. This is a sizing estimate, not a hard deadline.

Generator widening (`src/widen.rs`, LLP 3000.035 §5.1): from a stream
independent of the base generator (`generate::base`; a seed it leaves alone is
the base case byte for byte), cases gain prefetch-reachable reads, mutation
blocks (lets, `require`, `if`, several writes to `key` and `k2`, reads after
writes), a prelude of mutations `prep0..` run in order on the same store before
`run` (`Case::steps`, sometimes as the other viewer; `observe` then reads both
keys), and expiry (`expire at .until`, a clock advancing from `seeded_at`
through each step to `clock`, sweeps, `until: now + ttl`). Every oracle runs the
prelude (`execute::world`) and compares its outcomes (`prelude-*` fields;
`store-postgres-prelude`); a device acquires at the seed clock and catches up by
one sync round after the server's prelude. The prelude is emitted first so no
rewrite moves its sites. `lean::generate` narrows half the cases outside the
model (projections, expiry) back into its fragment; the rest stay named
unsupported (`shape`, `loop`, `table-expiry`). The report's `coverage` counts
each new feature (`block/*`, `prelude/*`, `projection/*`, `expire/*`, `clock/*`).

Quarry device testing (`src/devices.rs`, `src/device_writes.rs`, LLP 3000.035
§5.1 oracle 4): each case runs on a server engine and on a device of each store
(native SQLite at a file path, and memory), both brought up through the client's
own path: the `/schema` backend as JSON text, then `local.ts::syncRound` (capture
`sync_state`, `/sync` snapshot pages built as `serve_sync.rs` builds them,
`observe_store`, `apply` through `call_text`, catch-up stream). Never seed a
device store directly. Reads the device publishes without the server (`complete`
with `unknown` false, or a known refusal) compare the whole outcome record
except `seq` (and server-only `delivery`); every other read counts under a named
reason (`online-only-*`). Low ceilings reach the device only through `call`'s
`spent` ledger (`default - n`), compared at the server's boundary positions.
Writes go through `admit`: a device refusal must equal the server's, a
predicted row must equal the committed one (less its `pending` marker), and
after `settle` plus a sync round the device must answer `observe` as the server
does. `run-devices` accepts `--seconds`. Known findings are retained under
`cases/found/device-*` with a `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/devices.rs` replays them
and `tests/smoke.rs` pins the fixed-seed signature set.

The Lean oracle (`run-lean`, LLP 3000.035 §5.2): `snapback4_lang::emit::lean`
translates each generated program's surface tree (never the binder's IR) to
the model's case JSON; `quarry-oracle` (`lean/Oracle.lean`, one process per
worker) answers one tagged outcome per line; the engine runs on SQLite at
default ceilings. Compared: kind, refusal code, data (tagged: an id is not a
text), `complete`, `next` presence, each side's own continuation, and a
mutation's `observe` reread. Not compared: meter fields and sites. Programs
outside the model's fragment are named unsupported reasons, never passes.
`lean::generate` adds Lean-only variants (per-effect rules, a hidden probe
target, nullable/id orders, residual filters) aimed at `lean/OPEN.md`. A
disagreement is a Rust defect, a model defect (only when a document
contradicts the model: fix the model, rerun `lean/check.sh`), or an open
point (record the engine's behaviour in `OPEN.md`); never change the model to
match the engine without a document. Findings live in
`cases/found/lean--*` with `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/lean.rs` replays them and
pins the open signature set. Lean-dependent tests skip loudly without the
built model; `SNAPBACK4_REQUIRE_LEAN=1` makes that a failure.

Quarry scheduled testing: prepare a separate clean clone whose current branch
can fast-forward to `origin/main`. The runner fetches/fast-forwards it and starts
its own socket-only evaluation cluster with `fsync=off`,
`synchronous_commit=off`, `full_page_writes=off`, `shared_buffers=1GB`, and
`max_connections=200`. It builds the Lean model's `quarry-oracle` first
(`lake build` in `lean/`; a host without `lake` fails the run), then runs the
SQLite, store, device and Lean campaigns on the selected
seed window, replays shrunk findings twice, and files/appends open `issues/*.md`
tickets. Only ticket changes are committed/pushed, with three fetch/rebase
retries. State and reports live outside Git under `--state-dir`; seeds advance
only after completed work and successful owned-cluster cleanup. Dirty/diverged
trees, overlap, setup failures, partial reports and failed publication refuse.
A crash can retain the lock and cluster directory; inspect `lock/owner.json`
and the run report. Never stop a cluster by process name.

`--dry-run --cases 32` writes previews and a separate `next-seed.dry-run.json`,
never commits or pushes. Dry runs may explicitly supply `--external-postgres
'host=... user=... dbname=...'`; that cluster is neither started nor stopped by
the runner. Ambient connections do not override normal private-cluster ownership.
The installer remains an operator action; do not invoke it during development.

The Lean oracle on a scheduled host (one-time, per user, no sudo): install
elan with `curl https://raw.githubusercontent.com/leanprover/elan/master/elan-init.sh -sSf | sh -s -- -y --default-toolchain none`.
The runner finds `lake` on `PATH` or in `~/.elan/bin`; the first `lake build`
downloads the toolchain pinned in `lean/lean-toolchain` (Lean 4.34.1, no
Mathlib), so the first scheduled run needs network. Check by hand with
`(cd lean && ./check.sh)` in the runner's clone. `run.json` records the pinned
toolchain and the oracle binary's digest.

llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:1:# LLP 3000.000.000: Retained storage and hot release activation
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:11:Staging store classification and real-user provisioning: [LLP 3000.000.000.001](./3000.000.000.001-staging-with-real-users.rfc.md) (STAGE).
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:13:## 1. Commission and scope
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:15:Charlie approved the ideal end-game release lifecycle and commissioned implementation on 2026-10-12. Implementer: this ROLL session, beginning that date. Wave 8 DT1 found additive rollback deletes new storage in all 50 day-two lanes; DT1a found unnamed enum conflicts; DT10 found deployment requires stopping serve. This supersedes the release-only removal semantics in LLP 3000.000's implementation follow-up. Development and device replacement keep their existing projection/reset semantics. No new publication authority or gate is introduced.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:17:## 2. Retain by default
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:19:Every release activation (deploy and rollback) retains removed durable application tables, columns and index definitions in SQLite-owned dormant storage within the same activation transaction. The active r/i image remains an exact projection of the active schema. Dormant data is outside that namespace and unavailable to programs, rules, references, queries, sync, SQL application projections and clients. The Quarry/IR binder admits only declared application table names; it has no general application SQL evaluator. Dormant internal names are not added to Schema, and schema validation forbids declarations in the reserved `_dormant_` and `_release_` namespaces. Inspection `snapback4 sql` is owner-only and may enumerate it. A future application SQL adapter must keep its declared authorized input allow-list. Asset reads must resolve the home table, column and row in the active schema/r image and check the current read rule; a dormant home refuses E_ASSET_UNKNOWN even when a caller knows its id. Test the reserved-name/program boundary and direct fetch by id. Reintroducing a dormant name deliberately reactivates its data, after full validation under the candidate schema. A deployment that reuses any dormant name refuses unless --restore-dormant explicitly selects restoration; its refusal lists retained items/counts/samples and the alternatives `deploy <dir> --restore-dormant` or `prune <dir> --allow-destructive` before fresh creation. Rollback's selection of an immutable older release explicitly selects restoration; `rollback <dir> --to N` also selects an immutable release for deliberate roll forward. To convert durable to ephemeral, deploy a release without that table, prune it explicitly, then deploy the ephemeral definition. This applies to deployments as well as rollback; a rename remains two names, without an inferred data move.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:21:Inventory and audit entries carry a stable typed key (`table:name`, `column:table.name`, or `index:table.name`); pagination uses the returned key, not a bare path that could collide between columns and indexes. The SQLite format is `_dormant_tables(table_name, definition)` for whole-table Table definitions, `_dormant_rows(t,id,data)` for their stored images, `_dormant_columns(table_name,column_name,definition)` for retired column type/default/ownership, `_dormant_values(t,id,column_name,data)` for their cells, `_dormant_indexes(table_name,index_name,definition)` for index definitions and `_dormant_refs(t,id,column_name,parent_t,parent_id,invalid)` for deletion evidence with a parent lookup index. Catalog numbers are reserved monotonically and reused for the same physical name, but dormant rows are never kept in r under a hidden catalog number. These tables are created only during successful release activation; legacy deployments start empty and cannot recover data previously deleted by older binaries. A successful release activation writes the release-only format marker `version=snapback4-release-store-3`. New Store open/version checks accept this, the prior snapback4-release-store-2 and the legacy snapback4-store-1; old binaries reject it with E_STORE rather than opening it. Development and device stores retain snapback4-store-1 and their formats are unchanged. The format marker rolls back with failed activation. On restore, omitted cells from whole-table images move to dormant column cells before projection, merging previous retired cells; full-table entries are consumed only on commit.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:41:Partial restoration consumes only candidate-visible cells: B's T(a,b) → absent T → C's T(a) restores a and moves b to dormant cells, with b's definition, then B restores b. For an identical (t,id,column) cell key, the newly retired active value wins: it is the most recent authoritative value. Preserve other dormant keys. Use transactional upsert and test retire → restore → update → retire → restore. Keep zero-row table/column/index definitions too. Whole-table restoration first restores its stored image, splits omitted cells into dormancy, then projects the active image. Consumption shares commit with validation. Refuse application-table to derived-table ownership changes, or authoritative-column to maintained/captured ownership changes, including when the application table/column was dormant. Derived caches never overwrite retained authoritative data. The refusal names the incompatible owner and directs a deliberate rename or prune while dormant.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:43:At retirement record reference edges for dormant rows/cells (including optional/list references), bound to their current parent lifetime. An active delete transaction marks matching dormant edges invalid before the id can be reused, even for identical replacement values. Only a move into dormant storage or replacement of a derived cache is exempt from deletion invalidation. Actual authoritative-row deletion always invalidates matching dormant edges, including expiry during activation; failed activation rolls the invalidation back. A fixed-size identity migration rewrites dormant whole rows, cells, narrowed rows and their edge ledger when the referenced derived target lifetime survives migration; removing that target retires its lifetime, so its dormant edges remain bound to the retired address and restoration refuses rather than attaching them to a later reintroduction. Restoration refuses invalidated edges or missing parents with table/column/parent ids, counts and samples; it cannot silently recapture another parent's audience. For read-through rows, compare preserved captured audience and identity fields to the candidate parent before existing recapture work. A changed audience refuses restoration; inherited deadlines keep their original capture. Newly expired rows remain logically invisible and may be swept only under the reactivated schema. Test deletion/reuse, identical replacement, changed audience, nested references and expiry. This adds no device incarnation format.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:45:Restoration, candidate validation, derived rebuild, catalog/generation/programs and current release commit together. Any error rolls all of them back. Retained values overwrite no explicit candidate writes: restoration precedes activation builders. Incompatible name reuse must pass candidate types and constraints; no flag bypasses validation.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:47:## 3. Older writes and incompatible values
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:49:Updating an active row while its newer columns are dormant preserves those dormant values. Deleting that row deletes its dormant column values in the same transaction: forward never resurrects a deleted row, including when its id is reused. New rows written under the older schema have no dormant value. On forward they receive the candidate literal default or optional null; required columns without such a default refuse with exact missing-row counts and up to five ids. Repair by deploying a bridge schema with that column optional or a literal default using --restore-dormant, fill it through a bridge mutation, then deploy the desired required schema. Alternatively explicitly delete the named rows through the current release and retry. Never tell the older schema to write a field it lacks. Contextual now/viewer defaults do not invent historical values. Existing required-column backfill rules still apply.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:51:Release activations retain incompatible existing values per row rather than refusing a type narrowing. Section 9 specifies the visibility, reference and restoration rules. Development adoption keeps its checked narrowing behavior. Missing newly required fields still need the bridge described above; retention applies to present historical values, not absent backfill. No implicit coercion or fallback is introduced.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:55:## 4. Explicit pruning and audit
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:57:snapback4 prune <dir> runs through the serving owner when live, otherwise the offline owner. It lists dormant tables, columns and indexes with row counts, logical serialized dormant bytes (including retained asset bytes separately) and at most five ids; it changes nothing without --allow-destructive. With that flag it permanently deletes exactly the currently dormant storage in one writer transaction, records a durable prune event, and leaves active rows/programs untouched. It releases dormant asset homes for the ordinary collector. Deploy/rollback --allow-destructive do not prune; direct users to the separate command. Prune intentionally deletes all dormant items as one reviewed storage boundary; per-item dependency-aware pruning is deferred because partial pruning can strand dormant references and asset homes. There is no implicit size or age collector: previews make storage growth visible without inventing a data-loss policy. After prune, reintroducing the schema gets empty tables and normal required-column/default behavior. Pruning is an irreversible operator action; the command output names every item dropped.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:59:Release activation events live in the database transaction with activation revision, operation, release/previous/generation, timestamp and retained/restored summary. Prune records its item summary in that transaction. Existing releases.jsonl and current remain checked convenience copies; post-commit filesystem errors explicitly say activation succeeded. History is never edited or rewound. Rollback picks an earlier immutable source, not a historical data snapshot.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:63:## 5. Hot activation and ownership
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:65:serve owns the database sidecar for its lifetime and publishes a Unix-domain release-control socket in the private per-user runtime directory (§5.1; directory 0700, socket 0600). Check peer credentials on both ends (Linux SO_PEERCRED, Darwin getpeereid), requiring the effective UID. Only the host filesystem owner has this capability; it is not an HTTP door, browser origin, bearer service seat or remote endpoint. Check filesystem objects before replacing a stale socket; never follow symlinks or replace a regular file. Use `.release-command` (a hardened private regular file, no-follow, single link) for the command flock and the deterministic runtime address (§5.1) for the control socket. lstat socket entries; only a same-owner socket may be removed while holding the exclusive store sidecar. Bound command JSON to 4096 bytes and response JSON to 64 KiB, with a 5-second input timeout and 120-second command response timeout. The trusted same-UID operator can directly edit source/database; malicious same-UID edits are outside the threat model, but ordinary source races are caught by a candidate content digest over source/native/worker files in the request and verified by the server. Numeric release id is the only source selector; never a caller path. Commands hold a separate private command flock while allocating/staging immutable release source. They acquire the store owner when stopped; E_STORE_BUSY with a running serve uses the local socket instead of opening another writer. Dev and an older/unreachable server continue to refuse with an exact stop/retry command.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:73:### 5.1 Path-independent control address
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:106:and rollback derive exactly the same address from durable deployment location.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:134:Darwin start serve, execute hot deploy and rollback without restarting, refuse
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:147:## 6. Devices and partitions
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:149:Every changed execution activation including A→B→A gets a fresh monotone semantic generation, never reuses a release number as a generation. Execution-content identity includes the prepared native module digest as well as schema/programs. A changed native digest advances the existing semantic generation atomically with release activation, even when native declarations are unchanged; removing a module changes the digest too. Equal execution content retains generation unless a release activation changes the final active/dormant population (§9.1), which requires a new replica generation; source-only activations still advance activation revision. The digest is SHA-256 over the exact prepared wasm bytes; the no-module sentinel is the empty string persisted as release_native_digest. activation_hash binds schema/programs and that digest. Comparison is with the current activation, so A→B→A is fresh. Startup attaches the persisted execution identity and verifies the immutable release module matches its recorded digest. This uses the existing device protocol, not a separate native epoch. Rollback and forward use the same generation replacement and full partition reacquisition already used by adoption: /schema advertises the exact active shape, sync rejects old watermarks, devices clear obsolete base/index state and rebase or refuse their queued intents under the existing authority/operation contract. Store id remains unchanged; this is not a store replacement. No dormant rows or fields enter snapshots, partition logs, online answers or device stores. Never combine old-generation pages with a new backend. A late old read remains tagged old and cannot overwrite a newly observed generation. Simulate rollback/forward across a delayed sync page and audience changes; retained data changes server storage only, not device persistence formats.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:153:## 7. Implementation and acceptance
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:157:CLI matrix: `deploy <dir> [--restore-dormant] [--allow-feed-adoption]`; `rollback <dir> [--to N] [--allow-feed-adoption]`; `prune <dir> [--allow-destructive]`; `releases <dir>`. Legacy --allow-destructive on deploy/rollback is accepted but does not prune or bypass validation, and prints the prune command when relevant. --restore-dormant authorizes only restoration, never loss. --to requires a positive existing immutable release id and may select a newer release. E_ADOPT operator text: `table.column/index: N affected rows; sample ids [...]; values/keys [...]; reason; rewrite: <bridge/repair schema or command>; inspect: snapback4 sql <query> --release <dir>`. No flag bypasses invalid values, ownership or references. Unreachable live control refuses `E_STORE_BUSY: stop snapback4 serve <dir>, rerun the exact command, then snapback4 serve <dir>` before send; after send use `releases <dir>` and explicit `rollback --to N` after inspecting the outcome, never an automatic deploy allocating N+1.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:161:Tests also cover private socket permissions/symlinks/peer credentials, racing commands and writes during validation, process crash before commit and between commit/publication/reply, live prune/status, legacy empty dormancy and unchanged device format. Tests: deploy v1/write, v2 adding table/required default/enum/index/write, precise refusal while new enum is in use, authorized repair then rollback with dormant table/column, old update/insert/delete, forward restores prior values and supplies defaults only to new rows. Also prune preview/confirmation and asset lifetime; unique failure after dormant-index writes; required default-less forward refusal; transaction failure leaves active and dormant data intact; restart binds the committed release; simultaneous hot requests and failed hot activation; stale-generation sync and real client reacquisition; simulator delayed pages/audiences. Quality bar: the commissioned core/lang/device/sim cargo suites, CLI release/serve/adoption tests, relevant client tests and ref-check. No data-loss claim rests on diagnostics alone.

llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:173:The host implements the private same-owner transport, prepared native/worker custody, atomic live engine publication, reader fencing, revision-bound worker preparation, named-worker mutation and database-authoritative crash recovery. Seven focused hot-activation tests and four transport tests pass; the complete host unit suite passes (393 tests plus four existing ignored). All 23 release CLI/client integration tests pass, including an already-connected native-only client, concurrent traffic, hot narrowing refusal and process death before/after commit. An older release may make a through reference mutable; restoration refuses if its current parent table/id differs from the retained binding. SERVER.md owns the lifecycle semantics, the evolution card supplies the schema bridge, and the OPS deploy card is a one-line pointer. Final full quality results and commits are recorded in WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md. No publication is claimed.

llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:211:### Control socket portability qualification (2026-10-12)
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:238:## 9. Wave 9 release lifecycle amendment
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:242:### 9.1 Per-row narrowed-value dormancy
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:244:Each actual release activation, including roll-forward and equal-schema/program-only/native-only selection, runs the row-visibility closure. A visibility change advances generation even if execution hashes are equal, so devices replace their population; identical-source deploy no-ops do not activate. The closure removes authoritative rows whose **present** values do not fit the candidate column type or text constraint (asset leaves include ledger-backed kind, byte bound and file MIME allow-list, recursively through optionals/lists) into `_dormant_narrow_rows(t,id,data,definition,reason)`. Store format advances to `snapback4-release-store-3`; this binary opens format 2 and upgrades only during committed activation. Absent new required fields remain E_ADOPT. Engine metadata and maintained cache fields are not a narrowing trigger. Store the full authoritative image and its source table definition (merge restored whole-table and column definitions before classifying restored values), with interpreted old literal defaults and nulls, before projection. Restore any retained image that fits the candidate, splitting omitted columns into existing dormant cells. Restoration is automatic even on deploy; this is an existing row lifetime, not reuse of a removed schema name. No fallback syntax ships: the declared policy is hidden rows, never value substitution.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:246:Visibility is a fixed point: a row with a non-null reference (including nested/list references) to a dormant narrowed row is also dormant. This covers authorization parents and memberships. Active programs, rules, point reads, lists, counts, caps, maintained views, assets and application SQL see only active r/i. Tentative restore-and-rehide work does not count as a visibility change; compute population change from newly hidden rows and successfully restored rows in the final closed population. All dependent physical indexes and aggregates rebuild when rows enter or leave dormancy, including otherwise unchanged tables. Sync generation replacement clears base/index images and acquisition, then reacquires only that same active population. This supersedes the device literal-default image-retention optimization: an additive default can restore a dormant cell whose actual value is incompatible, so schema shape alone cannot certify an old image. Same-generation attachment and identical-source no-ops preserve images; outbox custody survives generation replacement. HAND owns the shipped-device compatibility floor; DX owns updates to client/evolution guide text. A reference lookup to a dormant id is absent. Preserve LLP 3000.039 §1/§4.4: references validate a nominal id, not target existence. A new write may carry a dormant id just as it may carry an unknown/hidden id; read-through or explicit require/insert rules decide target admission. Such a write remains active under the old release until the next adoption's reference-closure pass, but grants no parent-derived authority. Never probe dormancy selectively at the reference write boundary, which would be an existence oracle. Dormant rows contribute neither to uniqueness nor caps. Restoration uses the same set-null cap exemption as ordinary writes; null-exempt groups are never counted by adoption or successor assessment. Their ids remain reserved: inserts cannot replace them and return the same E_RULE as a hidden live identity conflict, without row contents or a special dormant code. Updates/deletes by old programs see an absent row; active updates retain unrelated dormant cells. Dormant uniqueness keys are not reserved: old writes may cause a constraint conflict on forward, which E_ADOPT reports with executable application SQL; resolve that conflict under the current release when those rows are active. If both conflict rows are dormant, deploy a bridge with compatible widened types and the conflicting index non-unique/cap increased using --restore-dormant, restore both, repair with authorized mutations, then reinstate the constraint. Neither row silently wins; activation refuses atomically. Diagnostics include this bridge and application SQL, with values only in privileged operator output (§3). Type incompatibility alone never blocks switching.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:248:At one pinned activation time, retire affected expired predecessor read-through rows under their old deadlines before narrowed hiding; retirement uses the ordinary authoritative delete to invalidate dependent evidence and remove dormant cells. Then restore candidate-compatible images, hide the incompatible/reference-dependent population, rebuild all physical r/i and _cap_counts/maintained caches, and validate before publication. Counts, sums and private parent-column mirrors all use this order. Reference closure removes at least one active row per iteration, terminates on the finite store even with nominal cycles, and any non-null element of a nested/list reference can hide its row. Null has no edge; a missing ordinary nominal target alone does not trigger hiding, while an absent target schema or narrowed-dormant target does. Capture dormant reference lifetime evidence before hiding; hiding is not deletion. Actual parent deletion invalidates evidence. Transferring a retained whole row or cell into narrowed dormancy preserves its existing bound/unbound/deleted evidence; it must never recapture a replacement parent. On candidate restoration, evidence invalidated by an actual deletion keeps a row dormant only if its reference/capture column is candidate-visible. An initially dangling nominal keep reference captures invalid=2 (unbound), distinct from invalid=1 (deleted captured lifetime); it may restore without inventing a lifetime. Deletion invalidates only bound (invalid=0) edges. Omitted reference cells and evidence stay retained for future restoration. A narrowed row never rebinds a deleted visible parent. Restored read-through images retain their historical captures and must pass the existing audience/deadline safety check. Whole-table restoration keeps the full source image through lifetime retirement and narrowed classification; ordinary candidate projection follows that boundary. Before projecting a narrowed image, evaluate retirement against its retained source definition/deadline, including direct expiry without read-through; removing read-through or expiry cannot revive an expired source lifetime. Actual retirement deletes the narrowed image, dependent captured evidence and its retained asset homes. Rows whose target table is absent stay dormant. Dormant rows run no jobs/expiry until reactivation. Assets remain retained but inaccessible. `prune --allow-destructive` drops narrowed images, associated dormant cells and lifetime evidence and releases only their asset homes; the preview names counts/bytes/sample ids. Inventory uses `rows:<table>` typed keys and audit items record before_rows, after_rows, restored_rows and bounded before_sample_ids in the activation transaction, including partial column/row restorations whose typed key remains. Per-table narrowed restoration counters distinguish restore-and-hide swaps with equal totals; summary restored_items includes partial restorations. Restoration identity sets are held transactionally and counted against final surviving r only after all read-through/cache activation phases. Column restored counts are recorded from cells actually consumed into surviving candidate rows, not inferred from inventory decreases; expiry/deletion may remove cells without restoring them. There is no automatic collection.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:250:### 9.2 Exact preview and operator vocabulary
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:252:`rollback <dir> --to N --check` and `check --store <path> --release N` execute candidate adoption on a private SQLite snapshot created by VACUUM INTO through a read-only source connection. This is disk-backed, uses the exact adoption builder/validation, and never changes the live source, pointer, events or generation. SQLite supports VACUUM INTO on a read-only source; it acquires a coherent WAL read snapshot without the source writer lock. BUSY/disk failures refuse with retry advice, never fall back to copying sidecars. The temporary directory is owner-only and removed on success, refusal and ordinary unwinding. Process kill/crash may leave a private preview directory; operators can remove snapback4-release-preview-* leftovers when no preview uses them. The live format, including format 2, is never upgraded by preview. The snapshot may require disk/time proportional to the store; it provides a point-in-time plan, not a write freeze. The output enumerates the full candidate inventory and totals every narrowed population, not just the first sixteen status items; it includes any E_ADOPT refusal. Preview prepares the candidate native content identity but never starts workers or effects.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:254:Deploy warns when type widening (including image/video byte bounds and file MIME/byte sets) admits values the previous release refuses; rollback also warns when its selected target admits values the current release refuses. All E_ADOPT inspect SQL uses quoted application table identifiers accepted by `sql`, including Quarry names that are SQL keywords; internal dormant inventory is inspected through `releases`. `latest` denotes the highest complete immutable source release, independently of current release and monotone generation. Complete here means a numeric directory with nonempty source that compiles; empty permanently reserved crash directories are skipped. Native preparation still validates a selected release before activation. Packaging and comparing the content digest precedes allocating a numeric release. Identical packaged source is a no-op: no new release directory, activation revision, audit or generation. A no-op is not an activation and therefore does not advance §5's activation revision. Widening warnings go to stderr, are advisory, and do not change a successful exit status. `releases <dir>` adds a JSON latest field; `releases <dir> [N|latest]` adds selected source id/digest/currentness without changing the authoritative current identity or activating; `rollback --to latest` and preview `--release latest` accept it. Command completion follows in-memory publication and prints `release N is current as generation G`.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:256:### 9.3 HTTP admission and reporting
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:262:Default adoption output is one measured receipt line; phase/cache details are exposed with --verbose/--json. The check adoption reporter emits N_ADOPTION_PAUSE only when estimated work exceeds one second (explicit verbose/json retains the small-store estimate).
