### 20.12.1 Wave 14 development clock windows (KIT3, 2026-10-16)

Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-16. The ordinary scheduler and default clock advance
keep latest-window coalescing: production must not replay an unbounded backlog
of effects after downtime. Development tests can request `clock advance
--each-boundary` (HTTP `eachBoundary:true`) to admit every missed window in
chronological boundary order, with declaration order breaking ties. Jobs drain
in that order on the final advanced clock; their periodStart/periodEnd arguments
name historical windows, while `now` remains the final advanced time. This is backlog admission, not
a historical simulation: test period-dependent work using periodStart/periodEnd,
not now. To test job logic dependent on now at each boundary, issue separate
advances. Scheduled
actions are admitted in order at the final advanced time (their deadlines
begin at admission, never at a historical periodEnd); external workers retain
their normal async execution contract. First-seen/changed schedules only prime their current
boundary. Held schedules remain held. Boundary/job admission keeps the existing
atomic saved-boundary transaction; restart never replays an admitted boundary.

Preflight always validates report metadata, including with held/replay schedules;
the hold only suppresses boundary enumeration/admission. Under --each-boundary it enumerates at most 10,000 crossed windows in total across all schedules, before
moving time or leases. A conservative report-size estimate also refuses above
15 MiB, reserving 1 MiB for the outer clock/work envelope inside the host's
16 MiB JSON encoder (and the existing 64 MiB CLI HTTP exchange).
Retained job summaries project only bounded fields; error previews are capped
at 1024 UTF-8 bytes plus an ellipsis, with errorTruncated and the run id. The
preflight includes the worst JSON escaping cost for that preview per schedule;
full error/args/cursor payloads are never loaded into the report.
Larger requests refuse E_CLOCK_INPUT and advise a smaller
advance; this bounds both execution and the explicit report. Each schedule's
reply includes firedWindows and skippedBoundaries (periodStart, periodEnd and,
for each skipped job window, its literal `snapback4 run <job>` command).
Coalesced advances have no crossed-window cap: they execute only the latest window
per schedule, retain the exact skippedPeriods count, and preview the first 32
skippedBoundaries per schedule with skippedBoundariesOmitted naming the remainder.
Report preflight budgets that bounded preview independently of jump distance.
Calendar schedules use calendar previous/next, never fixed-duration division.
An incomplete drain reports the already advanced now/skewMs and a refusal;
shutdown specifically refuses non-retryable E_CLOCK_INTERRUPTED rather than
turning a stopped job/delivery loop into successful HTTP 200;
callers inspect it and never blindly retry an advance. Ordinary maintenance
continues queued work; `run <job>` can inspect a run and `reset --clock reset`
provides an explicit fresh-test recovery if the scenario should be restarted. The default retains
existing fired/periodStart/periodEnd fields and adds the explicit windows.

Successful dev reset, clock status and clock advance replies include `now` and
`skewMs` (served time minus sampled wall time). The existing durable timed-
disclosure floor participates in both reported now and window preflight; they
cannot describe an earlier raw process-clock value after a persisted restart.
This reports the existing authoritative floor without changing its lifetime law.
Existing offset fields remain.
Reset keeps its current time unless clock:reset was requested. Error replies
after a clock change include the same clock evidence. No serve time controls,
clock rewind, job authority or production scheduler change is introduced.

implementation at `c94694f263` received independent **READY** verdicts from
[Muse Spark 1.3](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-wave14-kit3-r8.muse.md) and a
[fresh Codex gpt-6.1-sol session](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-wave14-kit3-r8.codex.md).
The [identical blind brief](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-wave14-kit3-r8.brief.md)
and all earlier received rounds are retained with provenance. Later commits
`e0af692817` and `74ee49d4df` correct the Expo thread's proof scope, configured browser selection
in the test harness, and guide/census assertions and custom-Home qualification scope; they add no semantic contract.
### 20.12.2 Wave 15 clock evidence (KIT4, 2026-10-18)

Implementer: Codex. A coalesced replay command includes --port with the actual
registered development listener, including memory hosts. An each-boundary
reason names every fired window. offsetMs and clockOffsetMs report the exact
requested process offset (initial offset plus accepted advances; reset clears
it); skewMs remains the observed authoritative now minus sampled wall time.
An advance captures that now/wall pair before draining maintenance; time spent
draining cannot subtract from the reported skew. Schedule selection is also
anchored to that captured advance sample: synchronous maintenance cannot add
later wall-time windows or report their unadmitted intermediate periods as
fired. Jobs/actions are still admitted on the live advanced clock (including
elapsed drain time), so action deadlines start at actual admission, never at a
historical periodEnd. Ordinary ticks select their own live boundary as before;
wall-time boundaries reached during the drain remain for the next ordinary tick.
Thus ordinary elapsed milliseconds and refused advances cannot change the
reported configured offset. The durable disclosure floor still governs now.
Neither job authority, production scheduling nor expiry semantics change.

//! Point reads own authorization and provisional expiry until their answer emits.
// @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — the ordinary point read is `authorized_point`: row and
// field authorization precede result pricing, under the request's one physical
// allowance. 0.4.2's inherited-aggregate, parent-key and event-slot branches
// (LLP 3000.014 §5, LLP 3000.016 §2.2) sit above that boundary and share it.
use super::{runner::Runner, rules, read::PrefixProbe};
use crate::{error::{RuntimeError, MeterKind}, ir::Read, value::Value};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

fn unavailable_aggregate() -> Value { crate::value::object([("value", Value::Null)]) }

impl Runner<'_> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — logical guard facts do not promise that rule
    // expiry traversal short-circuits. Null points have no witness on any
    // replica; reject before occupancy, coverage or lifetime work. Maintained
    // null groups remain real groups, and population equality is unchanged.
    pub(super) fn null_unique_point(&self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> bool {
        key.contains(&Value::Null) && self.schema.maintain_for(table).is_none()
            && self.schema.table(table).and_then(|t| t.index(index)).is_some_and(|i| i.unique)
    }

    pub(super) fn created(&self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> bool {
        if self.null_unique_point(table, index, key) { return false; }
        self.created.contains_key(&(table.to_owned(), index.to_owned(), crate::value::canonical_json(&key)))
    }

    /// A point read: the row when it exists and the viewer may read it, else
    /// `null`. Hidden and absent are the same word on purpose.
    pub(super) fn get(&mut self, read: &Read) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
        if self.decoration_cache.is_some() { return self.decoration_get(read); }
        let value = self.get_uncached(read)?;
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if self.following_witnesses.is_some() && let Value::Row(fields) = &value {
            let bytes = 256 + read.table.len() as u64 + fields.keys().map(|f| f.len() as u64 + 32).sum::<u64>() + value.field("id")?.as_str()?.len() as u64;
            self.meter.heap(bytes)?;
            self.following_witnesses.as_mut().unwrap().push(super::following_history::projection::Witness { table:read.table.clone(),id:value.field("id")?.as_str()?.into(),fields:fields.keys().cloned().collect() });
        }
        Ok(value)
    }

    pub(super) fn get_uncached(&mut self, read: &Read) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        let mut emitted = false;
        self.schema.refuse_private(&read.table)?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.21 — an unfinished maintained value is unavailable, never an absent group coerced to zero.
        let maintained = self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table).is_some();
        if maintained { self.meter.require_uncapped()?; }
        let mut value = self.get_inner(read, &mut emitted)?;
        if maintained { self.meter.require_uncapped()?; }
        if emitted && self.meter.capped_at.is_none() { self.stamp_source(&read.table, &mut value)?; expiry.emit(); }
        Ok(value)
    }

    fn get_inner(&mut self, read: &Read, emitted: &mut bool) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
        let key = read.key.iter().map(|value| self.expr(value)).collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
        if self.null_unique_point(&read.table, &read.index, &key) { return Ok(Value::Null); }
        self.observe_sequence_selection(&read.table, &read.index, &key)?;
        let table = self.schema.table(&read.table).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {}", read.table)))?;
        self.read_tables.insert(read.table.clone());
        self.live_read_dependency(&read.table);
        self.rule_dependencies(&table.rules.read);
        self.meter.at(&read.site);
        self.aggregate_read_coverage(&read.table, &read.index, &key, &read.site)?;
        let Some(maintain) = self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table) else {
            let (value, price) = self.get_prepared(read, &key, emitted)?;
            self.meter.heap(price)?;
            return Ok(value);
        };
        // @ref LLP 3000.028#3.4 — a generation-wide acquired flag cannot lend another caller an empty owner partition.
        if self.device && maintain.own_keys.is_some() && !self.tx.current_group(&read.table, &read.index, &key)? {
            self.unknown_fact(&read.table, &read.index, &key, &read.site);
            self.components.note(|frame| frame.speculative = true);
            return Ok(Value::Null);
        }
        if let Some(message) = maintain.expiring_read_refusal(self.schema) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_MAINTAIN_READ_EXPIRE", message });
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — a point hit, miss or denial reserves the same reusable row scratch before discovering occupancy.
        let scratch = self.aggregate_point_scratch(maintain, &key);
        self.meter.heap(scratch)?;
        let result = (|| {
            self.read_charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
            self.meter.require_uncapped()?;
            self.get_prepared(read, &key, emitted)
        })();
        // Transfer only the sealed result to retained ownership. Keep the
        // high-water mark, work counters and any cut when releasing scratch.
        self.meter.used.heap_bytes = self.meter.used.heap_bytes.saturating_sub(scratch);
        let (value, price) = result?;
        self.meter.heap(price)?;
        Ok(value)
    }

    /// The answer and the sealed price of its retained image, charged by the caller
    /// once its scratch is released.
    fn get_prepared(&mut self, read: &Read, key: &[Value], emitted: &mut bool) -> Result<(Value, u64), RuntimeError> {
        let table = self.schema.table(&read.table).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {}", read.table)))?;
        let index = table.index(&read.index).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing index {}.{}", read.table, read.index)))?;
        let maintained = self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table);
        let inherited = maintained.filter(|m| self.schema.tables[&m.source].through.is_some());
        let mut key_admitted = false;
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — only key-determined inputs may authorize a synthetic aggregate row.
        if let Some(m) = inherited.filter(|m| m.parent_key.is_some() || rules::deciding_fields(&read.table, table, &table.rules.read).iter().all(|field| index.columns().iter().take(key.len()).any(|column| column == field) || (field == "id" && m.group_fields().iter().all(|f| index.columns().iter().take(key.len()).any(|column| column == f))))) {
            let visible = (|| Ok(if let Some(parent) = &m.parent_key {
                let Some(at) = index.columns().iter().position(|f| f == &parent.reference) else { return Err(RuntimeError::Structural {code:"E_READ_THROUGH_SCAN",message:format!("{} must be read by its full parent key; use {}[parent] (guide maintains)",read.table,read.table)}); };
                let parent_id = key.get(at).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input("read through aggregate needs its full parent key".into()))?;
                match self.parent_verdict(&m.source,parent,parent_id)? {
                    super::through::ParentVerdict::Readable => true,
                    super::through::ParentVerdict::Unknown => { self.unknown_fact(&read.table,&read.index,key,&read.site); false },
                    _ => false,
                }
            } else {
                let mut fields: BTreeMap<_, _> = crate::small_map::collect(index.columns().into_iter().zip(key.iter().cloned()));
                let group_fields = m.group_fields();
                if group_fields.iter().all(|f| fields.contains_key(f)) {
                    let group: Vec<_> = group_fields.iter().map(|f| fields[f].clone()).collect();
                    fields.insert("id".into(), Value::Id(crate::schema::maintained_id(&group)));
                }
                let row = Value::Row(fields.into());
                self.meter.charge(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read))?;
                let viewer = self.context.viewer.clone();
                if self.device { self.authorize_read(&read.table, &row, true)? }
                else if self.context.is_system() { true }
                else {
                    // The key rule's probes were charged above, possibly to a cut; its
                    // witnesses still run under the request's physical allowance and
                    // the cut stops the read before any aggregate state is fetched.
                    self.prepaid_witnesses(|runner, allowance| rules::evaluate(&table.rules.read,&viewer,None,&row,false,&mut PrefixProbe { runner, allowance }))?
                }
            }))();
            if !visible? || self.read_capped() { return Ok((unavailable_aggregate(), 0)); }
            key_admitted = true;
            if self.device && (table.residency.online_only || (m.parent_key.is_none() && !self.captured_aggregate_coverage(m, index, key)?)) {
                self.unknown_fact(&read.table, &read.index, key, &read.site);
                return Ok((unavailable_aggregate(), 0));
            }
            if self.device && m.parent_key.is_some() {
                // A locally synthesized total has no settled row to freeze.
                // @ref LLP 3000.022#14.3 — never recount provisional receipt inputs.
                let group = self.receipt_read_group(m, &read.index, key)?;
                if self.beyond_horizon || self.receipt_maintain_pending(m, group.as_deref())? { return Ok((unavailable_aggregate(), 0)); }
                // The parent-key view is derived locally from fully held children;
                // its generated table need not have an invertible sync audience.
                let fields = crate::small_map::collect(index.columns().into_iter().zip(key.iter().cloned()));
                let row = self.live_aggregate_inner(m, Value::Row(fields.into()))?.unwrap_or(Value::Null);
                *emitted = true;
                // Key-authorized and computed from the caller's key and the disclosed total: sealed without a lookup.
                let sealed = self.authorized_synthetic(table, row);
                let price = sealed.heap_bytes() + sealed.projection_heap() + sealed.materialization_heap();
                return Ok((sealed.into_value(), price));
            }
        }
        if self.filter_depth > 0 && !self.read_charge(MeterKind::RuleProbes, 1)? { return Ok((Value::Null, 0)); }
        if self.device && table.sync.is_none() {
            self.unknown_fact(&read.table, &read.index, key, &read.site);

use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
struct Snapshot(PathBuf);
impl Drop for Snapshot {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);
    }
}

//! A row-read verdict and field projection together authorize result pricing.
use super::runner::Runner;
use crate::{error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError}, ir::Read, meter::Meter,
    schema::{Effect, Index}, value::{Disclosed, Value}};

// Only successful row authorization in this module can mint this evidence.
pub(crate) struct RowAuthorization(());
pub(crate) struct AuthorizedRow<'a> {
    projection: Disclosed<'a>,
    authority: RowAuthorization,
    // @ref LLP 3000.014#5 — a row's witness deadline is provisional until an answer emits it.
    expiry: super::expiry::Pending,
}
impl AuthorizedRow<'_> {
    pub(super) fn expiry(&self) -> super::expiry::Pending { self.expiry }
    pub(super) fn scalar_heap(&self, field: &str) -> u64 { self.projection.scalar_heap(field, &self.authority) }
    pub(crate) fn heap_bytes(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.heap_bytes(&self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn projection_heap(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.projection_heap(&self.authority) }
    pub(crate) fn materialization_heap(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.materialization_heap(&self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn into_value(self) -> Value { self.projection.into_value() }
    pub(super) fn into_parts(self) -> (Value, RowAuthorization) { (self.projection.into_value(), self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn charge(self, meter: &mut Meter) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
        meter.heap(self.heap_bytes() + self.projection_heap() + self.materialization_heap())?;
        Ok(self.into_value())
    }
}

impl RowAuthorization {
    pub(super) fn project(self, projection: Disclosed<'_>) -> AuthorizedRow<'_> { AuthorizedRow { projection, authority:self, expiry: Default::default() } }
}

impl<'r> Runner<'r> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — member reads share the row-read and field-disclosure capability.
    pub(super) fn authorized_member(&mut self, name: &str, id: &str, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] trusted: bool) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        // @ref LLP 3000.024#decision — this id came from an authorized head in
        // this read-only transaction. Price its public image before decoding,
        // then recheck the rule with a separate witness reservation. Neither
        // hidden/expired images nor private fields can select actual-size admission.
        #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
        if trusted && !self.device && !table.read_lifetime_sensitive() && table.recorded.is_empty()
            && table.through.is_none() && table.written_by.values().all(|a|a.audience) {
            if !self.read_charge(MeterKind::Examined,1)? { return Ok(None); }
            self.meter.heap(crate::rows::word_heap::text(id) + crate::rows::word_heap::slots::<Value>(1))?;
            let key = [Value::Id(id.into())];
            let Some((Some(row),_)) = self.scan_authorized(name,crate::schema::BY_ID,&key,&Default::default(),crate::rows::Dir::Asc,1,true,true,None)?.pop() else { return Ok(None); };
            let materialization = row.materialization_heap();
            let prepay = self.meter.mark();
            self.meter.heap(materialization)?;
            let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
            let row = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read), &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes,super::rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read),
                )], |runner,_| {
                let row = runner.authorize_image(name,row.into_value())?;
                if let Some(row) = &row { runner.next_expiry.inherit(row.expiry()); }
                Ok(row)
            });
            // The value now owns the default. Transfer its prepay to charge(),
            // keeping only one retained reservation after the recheck.
            self.meter.release_row_heap(prepay, materialization);
            let row = row?;
            if row.is_some() { expiry.emit(); }
            return row.map(|row|row.charge(&mut self.meter)).transpose();
        }
        let bytes = crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table).saturating_add(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read));
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        let row = self.authorized_scratch(bytes, &[(MeterKind::Examined, 1), (MeterKind::RuleProbes, super::rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read),
                )], |runner, scope| {
            let Some(mut row) = runner.tx.get(scope, name, id)? else { return Ok(None); };
            crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
            let until = table.deadline(&row);
            let authorized = runner.authorize_image(name, row)?;
            if let Some(row) = &authorized { runner.next_expiry.inherit(row.expiry()); runner.next_expiry.note_option(until); }
            Ok(authorized)
        })?;
        if row.is_some() { expiry.emit(); }
        row.map(|row|row.charge(&mut self.meter)).transpose()
    }

    pub(super) fn authorize_image(&mut self, name: &str, row: Value) -> Result<Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — a denied or expired candidate's witness deadlines are discarded with its scope.
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        if self.inactive(name, &row)? { return Ok(None); }
        if !self.check_rule(name, Effect::Read, None, &row)? {
            self.hidden_row();
            return Ok(None);
        }
        // Memory deadlines are hidden by Disclosed itself; transfer the live
        // boundary before projecting, still inside this candidate's expiry scope.
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() { self.note_expiry(table, &row); }
        let projection = self.disclosed_row(table, row).for_query();
        let visible = projection.into_value();
        Ok(Some(AuthorizedRow { projection: self.disclosed_row(table, visible).for_query(), authority: RowAuthorization(()), expiry: expiry.defer() }))
    }

    pub(super) fn scan_disclosed(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize) -> Result<Vec<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        self.scan_authorized(name,index,prefix,bounds,dir,limit,true,true,#[cfg(feature = "server-search")] None)
    }

    pub(super) fn scan_authorized(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize, metered: bool, trusted: bool, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] fields: Option<&[String]>) -> Result<Vec<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        self.scan_authorized_prepaid(name, index, prefix, bounds, dir, limit, metered, trusted, false, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] fields)
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.7 — a delivered uniform prefix has prepaid its
    // logical rule once; provisional rows still undergo physical authorization.
    pub(super) fn scan_authorized_prepaid(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize, metered: bool, trusted: bool, rule_prepaid: bool, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] fields: Option<&[String]>) -> Result<Vec<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        // A public, non-expiring image is authorized before accessing storage.
        // No hidden candidate can influence its actual-size decoder admission.
        // Trusted selected heads may use the same actual-size adapter. It
        // decodes a full transient image; the streaming consumer retains only
        // its ordering key. Selection remains useful in the private decoder.
        if (self.context.is_system() || trusted && !table.read_lifetime_sensitive()) && table.recorded.is_empty() && table.through.is_none() && table.written_by.values().all(|a| a.audience) {
            if metered { self.meter.heap(crate::rows::word_heap::slots::<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'_>>,Vec<u8>,
                )>(limit))?; }
            #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
            let live = table.read_lifetime_sensitive();
            // A web device never reads as the system, the only reader of an expiring table here.
            #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
            let live = false;
            let rows = if live { self.live_store_scan(name, index, prefix, bounds, dir, limit, metered)? }
            else if metered {
                self.metered_scan(|runner, scope| {
                    runner.tx.scan_metered(scope,name,index,prefix,bounds,dir,limit,&mut runner.meter)
                })?
            } else { self.metered_scan(|runner, scope| {
                    runner.tx.scan(scope,name,index,prefix,bounds,dir,limit)
                })? };
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.5 — a device prices the server's image with the server's charge. Its
            // own prediction rows still meet a non-uniform rule, as prepaid internal work outside the caller's

//! Release activation extends the existing engine transaction and generation.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#5-hot-activation-and-ownership — CAS includes every activation, not only semantic changes.
use super::*;
use rusqlite::OptionalExtension;

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct ReleaseActivation {
    pub release: u64,
    pub expected_previous: Option<u64>,
    pub expected_revision: Option<u64>,
    pub restore_dormant: bool,
    pub native_digest: Option<String>,
    pub operation: String,
}
impl ReleaseActivation {
    pub fn new(release: u64, expected_previous: Option<u64>) -> Self {
        Self {
            release,
            expected_previous,
            expected_revision: None,
            restore_dormant: true,
            native_digest: None,
            operation: "activate".into(),
        }
    }
    pub(crate) fn check(&self, conn: &rusqlite::Connection) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let actual: Option<String> = conn
            .query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key='release'", [], |r| {
                r.get(0)
            })
            .optional()?;
        if actual
            .as_ref()
            .is_some_and(|s| s.parse::<u64>().ok() != self.expected_previous)
        {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("current release changed; run `snapback4 releases <dir>` and retry the selected release".into()));
        }
        let revision: Option<String> = conn
            .query_row(
                "SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key='release_revision'",
                [],
                |r| r.get(0),
            )
            .optional()?;
        let revision = revision.and_then(|v| v.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0);
        if self.expected_revision.is_some_and(|n| n != revision) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("activation revision changed (including rollback/equal-content activation); run `snapback4 releases <dir>` and retry".into()));
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

impl Engine {
    pub fn new_release(
        store: Store,
        schema: Schema,
        programs: impl IntoIterator<Item = Program>,
        release: u64,
        expected_previous: Option<u64>,
    ) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        Self::new_release_with_feed_adoption(
            store,
            schema,
            programs,
            release,
            expected_previous,
            false,
        )
    }
    pub fn new_release_with_feed_adoption(
        store: Store,
        schema: Schema,
        programs: impl IntoIterator<Item = Program>,
        release: u64,
        expected_previous: Option<u64>,
        allow_feed_adoption: bool,
    ) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        Self::new_release_prepared(
            store,
            schema,
            programs,
            &ReleaseActivation::new(release, expected_previous),
            allow_feed_adoption,
        )
    }
    pub fn new_release_prepared(
        mut store: Store,
        mut schema: Schema,
        programs: impl IntoIterator<Item = Program>,
        options: &ReleaseActivation,
        allow_feed_adoption: bool,
    ) -> Result<Self, RuntimeError> {
        prepare(&mut schema)?;
        let programs = programs.into_iter().map(|p| (p.name.clone(), p)).collect();
        let (generation, metadata) = Self::activate(
            &mut store,
            &schema,
            &programs,
            Some(options),
            crate::now_ms(),
            allow_feed_adoption,
            None,
            None,
        )?;
        store.exclude_ephemeral(&schema);
        Ok(Self {
            timeline_schedule: timeline::Schedule::default(),
            ephemeral: std::sync::Arc::new(ephemeral::Hub::new(
                metadata.store_id,
                generation,
                &schema,
            )),
            store,
            schema,
            programs,
            generation,
            expiry_after: BTreeMap::new(),
            dev_effects: false,
            ceilings: Ceilings::default(),
            entropy: crate::ids::seed(),
            minter: crate::ids::Mint::default(),
            #[cfg(feature = "native")]
            native: None,
            #[cfg(feature = "native")]
            native_logs: native::Logs::default(),
            presence: None,
        })
    }
    /// No replacement engine, post-commit SQL read, or fallible ephemeral swap.
    pub fn adopt_release(
        &mut self,
        mut schema: Schema,
        programs: impl IntoIterator<Item = Program>,
        options: &ReleaseActivation,
        allow_feed_adoption: bool,
    ) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        prepare(&mut schema)?;
        let programs = programs.into_iter().map(|p| (p.name.clone(), p)).collect();
        drop(self.ephemeral.lock()?);
        let (generation, metadata) = Self::activate(
            &mut self.store,
            &schema,
            &programs,
            Some(options),
            crate::now_ms(),
            allow_feed_adoption,
            None,
            None,
        )?;
        self.store.exclude_ephemeral(&schema);
        self.timeline_schedule = timeline::Schedule::default();
        self.schema = schema;
        self.programs = programs;
        self.generation = generation;
        self.expiry_after.clear();
        self.dev_effects = false;
        self.ephemeral
            .rebind_committed(metadata.store_id, generation, &self.schema);
        Ok(generation)
    }
    #[cfg(feature = "native")]
    pub fn set_prepared_native(&mut self, module: Option<std::sync::Arc<native::Module>>) {
        self.native = module;
    }
}

fn prepare(schema: &mut Schema) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    columns::validate_before_prepare(schema)?;
    schema.check_row_view_audiences()?;
    schema.prepare_contribution_indexes();
    schema.prepare_delivery_lookup();
    if let Some(issue) = crate::schema::validate(schema).first() {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!(
            "{}: {}",
            issue.path, issue.message
        )));
    }
    Ok(())
}

    // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — row and field authorization precede result pricing.
    pub(super) fn authorized_point(&mut self, read: &Read, index: &Index, key: &[Value], prepaid: bool, trusted: bool, prepaid_examined: bool, absent: &mut bool) -> Result<Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[&read.table];
        let row = if crate::rows::disclosed::actual_size(table) {
            if !prepaid_examined && !self.read_charge(MeterKind::Examined,1)? { self.point_cut(read, key); return Ok(None); }
            let scratch = self.meter.mark();
            let row = self.metered_scan(|runner, scope| {
                    runner.tx.scan_metered(scope,&read.table,&read.index,key,&Default::default(),crate::rows::Dir::Asc,1,&mut runner.meter)
                })?.pop();
            self.meter.release_heap(scratch);
            let Some((mut row,_)) = row else {
                *absent = true;
                self.point_miss(read, key);
                #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
                if !self.device && !self.read_capped() { self.no_candidate(&read.table, &read.site); }
                return Ok(None);
            };
            if !self.point_event_held(read, key, &row) {
                return Ok(None);
            }
            if self.device && self.retained_row(&read.table,&row)? { self.components.note(|frame|frame.retained=true); }
            crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,table);
            row
        } else {
            let bytes = crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table).saturating_add(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read));
            // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — a hidden, expired or absent candidate's deadlines are discarded with this scope.
            let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
            let authorized = self.authorized_scratch(bytes, &[(MeterKind::Examined, u64::from(!prepaid_examined))], |runner, scope| {
                #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
                let mut encountered = false;
                let Some(row) = runner.live_lookup(scope,&read.table, &read.index, index, key, #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] Some(&mut encountered))? else {
                    *absent = true;
                    runner.point_miss(read, key);
                    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
                    if !encountered && !runner.device && !runner.read_capped() { runner.no_candidate(&read.table, &read.site); }
                    return Ok(None);
                };
                // @ref LLP 3000.016#21a-owned-events-follow-their-source-rows — a held source whose event kind is not fully synced answers nothing.
                if !runner.point_event_held(read, key, &row) {
                    return Ok(None);
                }
                if runner.inactive(&read.table, &row)? { return Ok(None); }
                let provisional = runner.is_provisional(&read.table, &row)?;
                let allowed = if provisional && !runner.context.is_system() {
                    runner.authorize_read(&read.table, &row, prepaid)?
                } else { trusted || runner.check_rule(&read.table, Effect::Read, None, &row)? };
                if !allowed { if !runner.read_capped() {runner.hidden_row();} return Ok(None); }
                // @ref LLP 3000.001#proposed-composition-of-retained-and-authoritative-reads — a delivered left point hit marks only its component, never whole-query coverage or a miss.
                if runner.device && runner.retained_row(&read.table, &row)? { runner.components.note(|frame| frame.retained = true); }
                runner.note_expiry(table, &row);
                let projected = runner.disclosed_row(table, row).for_query();

                let visible = projected.into_value(); // drop hidden storage before leaving the internal scope
                Ok(Some(AuthorizedRow { projection: runner.disclosed_row(table, visible).for_query(), authority: RowAuthorization(()), expiry: Default::default() }))
            })?;
            // The live aggregate is an authorized result computation. It must keep
            // its ordinary work budget and cannot publish a partial scalar value.
            let Some(authorized) = authorized else {
                if !*absent && self.read_capped() { self.point_cut(read, key); }
                return Ok(None);
            };
            expiry.emit();
            authorized.into_value()
        };
        // A maintained point read has already reserved its recount scratch (aggregate_point_scratch).
        let row = if prepaid_examined { self.live_aggregate_prepaid(&read.table, row)? } else { self.live_aggregate(&read.table, row)? }.unwrap_or(Value::Null);
        Ok(Some(AuthorizedRow { projection: self.disclosed_row(table, row).for_query(), authority: RowAuthorization(()), expiry: Default::default() }))
    }

    fn point_event_held(&mut self, read: &Read, key: &[Value], row: &Value) -> bool {
        if self.device && !crate::schema::timeline::holds_event(self.schema, &read.table, row) {
            self.event_read_unknown(&read.table, &read.index, key, &read.site);
            return false;
        }
        true
    }

    /// A key-authorized synthetic aggregate image (its fields are the caller's
//! Release backfills use the same activation transaction as retention and indexes.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#3-adoption-and-reversibility — stage every result before mutating the input snapshot.
use super::*;
use crate::{schema::{backfill::Backfill, materialize_optional_fields}, engine::{Context, runner::Runner}, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
pub(crate) const VERSION: &str = "snapback4-release-store-4";
fn refusal(message: impl Into<String>) -> RuntimeError { RuntimeError::Structural { code:"E_ADOPT", message:message.into() } }
fn present(tx: &Tx<'_>) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    Ok(tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name='_backfill_evidence')",[],|r|r.get(0))?)
}
fn enable(tx: &Tx<'_>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    tx.conn().execute_batch("CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _backfill_evidence(t INTEGER NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,definition TEXT NOT NULL,inputs TEXT NOT NULL,before_image TEXT NOT NULL,after_image TEXT NOT NULL,active INTEGER NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,id,definition)) WITHOUT ROWID;
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _backfill_pruned(t INTEGER NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,definition TEXT NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,id,definition)) WITHOUT ROWID;")?;
    tx.set_meta("version", VERSION)
}
#[derive(serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
struct Evidence { inputs: Value, before: BTreeMap<String,Value>, after: BTreeMap<String,Value> }
fn evidence(tx: &Tx<'_>, t:i64,id:&str,key:&str) -> Result<Option<Evidence>,RuntimeError> {
    let value:Option<(String,String,String)>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT inputs,before_image,after_image FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3",params![t,id,key],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?,r.get(2)?))).optional()?;
    value.map(|(inputs,before,after)|Ok(Evidence {inputs:decode(&inputs)?,before:decode(&before)?,after:decode(&after)?})).transpose()
}
fn decode<T:serde::de::DeserializeOwned>(s:&str)->Result<T,RuntimeError>{serde_json::from_str(s).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))}
fn save(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,key:&str,e:&Evidence,active:bool)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _backfill_evidence VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5,?6,?7) ON CONFLICT(t,id,definition) DO UPDATE SET inputs=excluded.inputs,before_image=excluded.before_image,after_image=excluded.after_image,active=excluded.active",params![t,id,key,canonical_json(&e.inputs),canonical_json(&e.before),canonical_json(&e.after),active])?;Ok(())
}
pub(super) fn delete(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    if present(tx)? {
        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id])?;
        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id])?;
    } Ok(())
}
// Never archive reference/asset preimages without lifetime evidence. New reference
// fields are safe: subsequent retirement is owned by the ordinary dormant ledger.
fn protected(kind:&crate::schema::ColumnType)->bool {
    use crate::schema::ColumnType::*;
    match kind {Optional(k)=>protected(k),Ref{..}|Image{..}|Video{..}|File{..}|List{..}|ListRange{..}=>true,_=>false}
}
pub(super) fn transition(tx:&Tx<'_>,next:&Schema,previous:Option<&Schema>,sources:&BTreeMap<String,Table>,now:i64,report:&mut super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    if next.backfills.is_empty() && previous.is_none_or(|s|s.backfills.is_empty()) { return Ok(()); }
    enable(tx)?;
    for fill in &next.backfills { fill.admit(next).map_err(refusal)?; }
    // A name is immutable within a continuous lineage. Replacing a transform
    // deliberately goes through an old-source/bridge transition first.
    if let Some(old)=previous {for f in &next.backfills {if old.backfills.iter().any(|p|p.table==f.table && p.name==f.name && p.key()!=f.key()) {return Err(refusal(format!("backfill {} {} changed; use a new name in a bridge release rather than replacing historical meaning",f.table,f.name)));}}}
    let next_keys:BTreeSet<_>=next.backfills.iter().map(Backfill::key).collect();
    if let Some(old)=previous {for fill in old.backfills.iter().rev().filter(|f|!next_keys.contains(&f.key())) {
        let Some(table)=next.table(&fill.table) else {continue};
        let t=tx.num(&fill.table)?;let key=fill.key();let mut bad=super::adopt_refusals::Samples::default();let mut count=0;
        super::activation::walk(tx,t,|id,mut row|{
            let Some(e)=evidence(tx,t,id,&key)? else {
                let pruned:bool=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3)",params![t,id,key],|r|r.get(0))?;
                if pruned && fill.outputs.iter().any(|c|table.columns.contains_key(c)) {bad.add(id,"rollback preimage was explicitly pruned; select a bridge preserving current data or restore a backup");}
                return Ok(())
            };
            let fields=match &mut row {Value::Row(f)|Value::Map(f)=>f,_=>return Err(refusal("invalid stored row"))};
            let mut changed=false;
            for c in &fill.outputs {if table.columns.contains_key(c) {
                let current=fields.get(c);let before=e.before.get(c);let after=e.after.get(c);
                if current!=after && current!=before {bad.add(id,&format!("{}.{} changed after backfill {}; keep a bridge and repair through an authorized mutation before rollback",fill.table,c,fill.name));return Ok(())}
                if let Some(value)=before {fields.insert(c.clone(),value.clone());}else{fields.remove(c);}
                changed=true;
            }}
            if changed {tx.conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data=?1 WHERE t=?2 AND id=?3",params![canonical_json(&row),t,id])?;count+=1;}
            save(tx,t,id,&key,&e,false)
        })?;
        if bad.count>0 {return Err(refusal(format!("backfill {} {} rollback: {}",fill.table,fill.name,bad.describe())))}
        if count>0 {report.backfills.push(format!("{} {} reversed on {count} rows",fill.table,fill.name));tx.set_meta("activation_population","true")?;}
    }}
    let old_keys:BTreeSet<_>=previous.into_iter().flat_map(|s|s.backfills.iter().map(Backfill::key)).collect();
    let entering:Vec<_>=next.backfills.iter().filter(|f|!old_keys.contains(&f.key())).collect();
    if entering.is_empty(){return Ok(())}
    let populated = entering.iter().try_fold(false, |any,fill| -> Result<bool,RuntimeError> {
        Ok(any || tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE t=?1)",[tx.num(&fill.table)?],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))?)
    })?;
    if !populated {return Ok(())}
    let mut source=previous.cloned().unwrap_or_default();source.tables=sources.clone();source.backfills.clear();
    // Point lookup indexes are rebuilt against the assembled source images,
    // including restored rows, before any output patch is applied.
    let mut reads=BTreeSet::new();
    for f in &entering {collect_reads(&f.program.body,&mut reads);}
    for (name,index) in reads {
        let wanted=next.table(&name).and_then(|t|t.index(&index)).ok_or_else(||refusal("backfill lookup index absent"))?;
        let old_table=source.tables.get_mut(&name).ok_or_else(||refusal(format!("backfill lookup source {name} has no historical definition")))?;
        if !wanted.is_plain() || wanted.columns().iter().any(|c|c!="id" && !old_table.columns.contains_key(c)) {return Err(refusal(format!("backfill lookup {name}.{index} requires predecessor fields; use an indexed bridge")))}
        old_table.indexes.insert(index.clone(),wanted.clone());
        let old=&source.tables[&name].indexes[&index];
        let ti=tx.num(&format!("{name}.{index}"))?;
        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM i WHERE ti=?1",[ti])?;
        super::activation::walk(tx,tx.num(&name)?,|id,mut row|{
            materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,&source.tables[&name]);
            for key in index_keys(old,&row,id) {tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO i(ti,k,id) VALUES(?1,?2,?3)",params![ti,key,id])?;} Ok(())
        })?;
    }
    tx.conn().execute_batch("CREATE TEMP TABLE _backfill_pending(t INTEGER NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,definition TEXT NOT NULL,patch TEXT NOT NULL,evidence TEXT NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,id,definition)) WITHOUT ROWID;")?;
    for fill in entering {
        let t=tx.num(&fill.table)?;let key=fill.key();let table=&next.tables[&fill.table];let mut bad=super::adopt_refusals::Samples::default();let mut count=0;
        super::activation::walk(tx,t,|id,mut row|{
            let prior=evidence(tx,t,id,&key)?;
            if prior.is_none() && previous.and_then(|s|s.table(&fill.table)).is_some_and(|t|fill.outputs.iter().all(|c|!t.columns.contains_key(c)))
                && fill.outputs.iter().all(|c|row.field(c).is_ok() && crate::engine::validate::validate_column(&fill.table,c,&table.columns[c],row.field(c).ok()).is_ok()) {return Ok(())}
            if let Some(table)=sources.get(&fill.table){materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,table);}
            let mut args=BTreeMap::new();
            for (c,kind) in &fill.inputs {
                let value=row.field(c).cloned().ok();
                if crate::engine::validate::validate_column(&fill.table,c,kind,value.as_ref()).is_err() {
                    // Native rows can survive a return through an older source;
                    // they have no historical input, but retain a valid v2 image.
                    if prior.is_none() && fill.outputs.iter().all(|c|crate::engine::validate::validate_column(&fill.table,c,&table.columns[c],row.field(c).ok()).is_ok()) {return Ok(())}
                    bad.add(id,&format!("historical input {}.{c} is absent or does not fit {kind}; use a typed bridge to repair it",fill.table));return Ok(())
                }
                args.insert(c.clone(),value.unwrap_or(Value::Null));
            }
            let inputs=Value::Row(args.clone().into());
            let mut context=Context::system().at(now);context.args=args;
            let mut runner=Runner::new(tx,&source,context,Meter::new(Ceilings::job(),Cut::Abort),0);
            let patch=match runner.run(&fill.program) {Ok(Value::Row(f)|Value::Map(f))=>f.into_iter().collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>(),Ok(_)=>{bad.add(id,"backfill did not return an object");return Ok(())},Err(e)=>{bad.add(id,&e.to_string());return Ok(())}};
            if runner.read_cut().is_some() {bad.add(id,"historical lookup did not complete; reduce per-row lookup work before adoption");return Ok(())}
            if patch.keys().cloned().collect::<BTreeSet<_>>()!=fill.outputs {return Err(refusal("backfill returned undeclared output fields"))}
            let fields=row.as_object()?;let mut before=BTreeMap::new();let mut desired=patch.clone();
            for (c,value) in &patch {
                if let Err(e)=crate::engine::validate::validate_column(&fill.table,c,&table.columns[c],Some(value)) {bad.add(id,&e.to_string());return Ok(())}
                if let Some(kind)=sources.get(&fill.table).and_then(|t|t.columns.get(c)) {if protected(kind) && prior.as_ref().is_none_or(|e|e.before.contains_key(c)) {bad.add(id,&format!("cannot overwrite historical reference or asset {}.{c}; derive into a new column",fill.table));return Ok(())}}
                if previous.and_then(|s|s.table(&fill.table)).is_some_and(|t|t.columns.contains_key(c)) || (previous.and_then(|s|s.table(&fill.table)).is_none() && sources.get(&fill.table).is_some_and(|t|t.columns.contains_key(c))) {
                    if let Some(v)=fields.get(c){before.insert(c.clone(),v.clone());}
                }
                if let Some(prior)=&prior {
                    let deps_same=prior.inputs==inputs && prior.after==patch;
                    let current=fields.get(c);
                    let original=prior.before.get(c);
                    if deps_same && original.is_none() && current.is_some() {
                        // Added columns retain edits under v1's dormant storage.
                        desired.insert(c.clone(),current.unwrap().clone());
                    } else if !deps_same && current!=prior.after.get(c) && current!=original {
                        bad.add(id,&format!("{}.{} was edited and its historical inputs changed; keep a bridge and resolve the conflict",fill.table,c));return Ok(())
                    }
                }
            }
            let e=Evidence{inputs,before,after:patch};
            tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO temp._backfill_pending VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5)",params![t,id,key,canonical_json(&desired),canonical_json(&e)])?;count+=1;
            Ok(())
        })?;
        if bad.count>0{return Err(refusal(format!("backfill {} {}: {}",fill.table,fill.name,bad.describe())))}
        if count>0{report.backfills.push(format!("{} {} transformed {count} rows",fill.table,fill.name));}
    }
    // Stream patches; avoid retaining the whole transformed population in RAM.
    let mut stmt=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT t,id,definition,patch,evidence FROM temp._backfill_pending ORDER BY t,id,definition")?;
    let mut rows=stmt.query([])?;
    while let Some(r)=rows.next()? {
        let t:i64=r.get(0)?;let id:String=r.get(1)?;let key:String=r.get(2)?;
        let patch:BTreeMap<String,Value>=decode(&r.get::<_,String>(3)?)?;let e:Evidence=decode(&r.get::<_,String>(4)?)?;
        let data:String=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT data FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id],|r|r.get(0))?;let mut row:Value=decode(&data)?;
        if let Value::Row(f)|Value::Map(f)=&mut row {f.extend(patch);}
        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data=?1 WHERE t=?2 AND id=?3",params![canonical_json(&row),t,id])?;
        save(tx,t,&id,&key,&e,true)?;
    }
    drop(rows);drop(stmt);
    tx.conn().execute_batch("DROP TABLE temp._backfill_pending;")?;
    // The source-index rebuild and every transformed dependency require full
    // candidate key/cap/maintain validation, even when its declaration is equal.
    tx.set_meta("activation_population","true")?;
    Ok(())
}
fn collect_reads(body:&[crate::ir::Stmt],out:&mut BTreeSet<(String,String)>){
    use crate::ir::{Expr,Stmt};
    fn expr(e:&Expr,out:&mut BTreeSet<(String,String)>){match e{
        Expr::Scan(r)=>{out.insert((r.table.clone(),r.index.clone()));for k in &r.prefix{if let Some(e)=k.scalar(){expr(e,out);}}if let Some(e)=&r.filter{expr(e,out);}},
        Expr::Get(r)|Expr::Exists(r)=>{out.insert((r.table.clone(),r.index.clone()));for e in &r.key{expr(e,out);}},
        Expr::Field{base,..}|Expr::Unary{value:base,..}=>expr(base,out),Expr::Array(es)|Expr::Builtin{args:es,..}=>{for e in es{expr(e,out);}},
        Expr::Object(fs)=>{for e in fs.values(){expr(e,out);}},Expr::Shape{base,fields}=>{expr(base,out);for e in fields.values(){expr(e,out);}},
        Expr::Binary{left,right,..}=>{expr(left,out);expr(right,out);},Expr::Block{body,value}=>{collect_reads(body,out);expr(value,out);},_=>()
    }}
    for s in body{match s{Stmt::Let{value,..}|Stmt::Const{value,..}|Stmt::Assign{value,..}|Stmt::Return(value)=>expr(value,out),Stmt::Require{condition,..}=>expr(condition,out),Stmt::If{condition,then_body,else_body}=>{expr(condition,out);collect_reads(then_body,out);collect_reads(else_body,out);},_=>()}}
}

// Prune forgets historical values, retaining only the fact that exact reversal
// is no longer available. No tombstone can be queried by an app.
pub(super) fn prune(tx:&Tx<'_>)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    if present(tx)? {tx.conn().execute_batch("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _backfill_pruned SELECT t,id,definition FROM _backfill_evidence; DELETE FROM _backfill_evidence;")?;}
    Ok(())
}

    /// The release's compiled authoring metadata must describe the persisted
    /// programs we attach to. Neither stale selection nor edited release
    /// source is permission to activate another backend during startup.
    // @ref LLP 3000.000#the-atomic-object-to-define — startup attaches to the persisted activation
    pub fn open_release_engine(&self, store: Store, bound: &Bound, release: u64, now: i64) -> Result<Engine, RuntimeError> {
        if store.meta("release")?.is_none() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("this deployment predates stored release activations; deploy its source once before serving".into()));
        }
        let engine = Engine::attach_release_at(store, release, now)?;
        let same_programs = bound.programs.len() == engine.programs().count()
            && bound.programs.iter().all(|bound| engine.program(&bound.program.name) == Some(&bound.program));
        if engine.schema() != &bound.schema || !same_programs {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("release source differs from its activated backend; deploy the source as a new release".into()));
        }
        Ok(engine)
    }
}

pub(crate) fn node() -> PathBuf {
    let pinned = std::env::var_os("HOME").map(PathBuf::from).map(|p| p.join(".volta/tools/image/node/24.18.0/bin/node"));
    std::env::var_os("SNAPBACK_NODE").map(PathBuf::from).or_else(|| pinned.filter(|p| p.is_file())).unwrap_or_else(|| PathBuf::from("node"))
}

//! Release backfills use the same activation transaction as retention and indexes.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#3-adoption-and-reversibility — stage every result before mutating the input snapshot.
use super::*;
use crate::{schema::{backfill::Backfill, materialize_optional_fields}, engine::{Context, runner::Runner}, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
pub(crate) const VERSION: &str = "snapback4-release-store-4";
fn refusal(message: impl Into<String>) -> RuntimeError { RuntimeError::Structural { code:"E_ADOPT", message:message.into() } }
fn present(tx: &Tx<'_>) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    Ok(tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name='_backfill_evidence')",[],|r|r.get(0))?)
}
fn enable(tx: &Tx<'_>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    tx.conn().execute_batch("CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _backfill_evidence(t INTEGER NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,definition TEXT NOT NULL,inputs TEXT NOT NULL,before_image TEXT NOT NULL,after_image TEXT NOT NULL,active INTEGER NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,id,definition)) WITHOUT ROWID;
    CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _backfill_pruned(t INTEGER NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,definition TEXT NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,id,definition)) WITHOUT ROWID;")?;
    tx.set_meta("version", VERSION)
}
#[derive(serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize)]
struct Evidence { inputs: Value, before: BTreeMap<String,Value>, after: BTreeMap<String,Value> }
fn evidence(tx: &Tx<'_>, t:i64,id:&str,key:&str) -> Result<Option<Evidence>,RuntimeError> {
    let value:Option<(String,String,String)>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT inputs,before_image,after_image FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3",params![t,id,key],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?,r.get(2)?))).optional()?;
    value.map(|(inputs,before,after)|Ok(Evidence {inputs:decode(&inputs)?,before:decode(&before)?,after:decode(&after)?})).transpose()
}
fn decode<T:serde::de::DeserializeOwned>(s:&str)->Result<T,RuntimeError>{serde_json::from_str(s).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))}
fn save(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,key:&str,e:&Evidence,active:bool)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _backfill_evidence VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5,?6,?7) ON CONFLICT(t,id,definition) DO UPDATE SET inputs=excluded.inputs,before_image=excluded.before_image,after_image=excluded.after_image,active=excluded.active",params![t,id,key,canonical_json(&e.inputs),canonical_json(&e.before),canonical_json(&e.after),active])?;Ok(())
}
pub(super) fn delete(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    if present(tx)? {
        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_evidence WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id])?;
        tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2",params![t,id])?;
    } Ok(())
}
// Never archive reference/asset preimages without lifetime evidence. New reference
// fields are safe: subsequent retirement is owned by the ordinary dormant ledger.
fn protected(kind:&crate::schema::ColumnType)->bool {
    use crate::schema::ColumnType::*;
    match kind {Optional(k)=>protected(k),Ref{..}|Image{..}|Video{..}|File{..}|List{..}|ListRange{..}=>true,_=>false}
}

    if next.backfills.is_empty() && previous.is_none_or(|s|s.backfills.is_empty()) { return Ok(()); }
    enable(tx)?;

**NOT READY**

Four material issues remain:

1. **Dormancy can apply a same-column transformation twice (§3).** Consider `n → n * 2`: v1 stores `3`, v2 transforms it to `6`, v3 removes the table and declaration, then v2 is restored. [backfills.rs](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:46) skips reversal when the destination lacks the table. Restoration then treats the declaration as entering and can transform `6 → 12`, replacing the original preimage. Specify declaration state per retained row lifetime, including while dormant. Re-entry must recover historical inputs without feeding a transformation its own output. Add this four-release regression and verify subsequent rollback restores `3`.

2. **Narrowed dormant rows enter after the backfill snapshot (§3, steps 1–4).** [dormant.rs](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/dormant.rs:576) runs backfills before narrowed-row restoration. A candidate that widens an old field and adds a required derived field therefore misses previously narrowed rows; they can subsequently restore without the required patch. Historical lookups also miss those rows. Explicitly include restoration-eligible narrowed rows in source assembly before evaluation, without requiring their unfinished candidate images to validate first. Test both direct transformation and lookup through such a restored row.

3. **Candidate uniqueness does not prove historical lookup uniqueness (§2).** The source-index builder in [backfills.rs](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:81) installs candidate index metadata without checking historical duplicate keys. Ordinary point execution retrieves one matching row. Final uniqueness validation is insufficient: another simultaneous patch could resolve the duplicates, allowing activation to succeed with an arbitrarily selected historical dependency. Require lookup cardinality validation against the assembled source snapshot, before patches. Duplicate historical points must refuse even when final candidate keys would be unique.

4. **Hot activation uses wall time instead of the staging clock (§§3–4).** [serve_release.rs](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/serve_release.rs:118) calls `adopt_release`, which pins `crate::now_ms()` in [engine/release.rs](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/release.rs:145). With a negative staging offset, a lookup row valid at served time can appear expired during migration; source retirement can likewise use the wrong time. Specify one authoritative activation-time sample, captured under the writer from the serving clock with its disclosure floor, and pass it through activation. Define preview clock sampling explicitly and test both offset signs.

The staging classification and service-session authorization boundaries appear consistent with §4. Static, read-only review; no tests executed.
